To exit: press **"Begin 7-day unlock delay"**, wait seven days, then return to the Portfolio page's **Pending withdrawals** and press **Withdraw**, which calls `claimLock(lockId)` on the UnstakingManager `0x6A56cdef9f23F3b05270AD116D6903CCE3181698`. **Unlocking is two transactions, seven days apart.** You can cancel a pending unlock and resume voting.

**What vote-lockers get, stated mechanically rather than as a yield.** Governance fee revenue is routed to the vault's **GenericTokenJar `0xEAfA84184BEb90891cb5c4942ebD59C18dda0bfE`**, whose `token()` is RSR on Base. The jar receives MAG7 shares from `distributeFees()`; a permissionless `createTrustedFill(...)` call converts them to RSR; the RSR lands in the vault and streams in at the 7-day half-life, **raising the vlRSR→RSR exchange rate**. There is no claim transaction and no reward-token registration — indeed RSR *cannot* be registered as a reward token, because the vault's guard rejects any reward token equal to its own `asset()`, which is why `getAllRewardTokens()` returns an empty array — and why that empty array should not be read as rewards being switched off. **The current state, checkable and worth stating candidly: as of 2026-09-16 the jar holds about 10.26 MAG7 and zero RSR, the conversion is permissionless and has not been run, and the vault's exchange rate is still exactly 1.000000 — so nothing has accrued to vlRSR to date.** This document prints no rate; see Section 12.4 for why no yield or performance figure appears anywhere in it.

**What vote-lockers can decide.** Everything on the standard path listed in Section 8.7 — and, on the fast path, one thing only (Section 8.10). Vote-locking is **not** staking-as-collateral: on an Index DTF locked RSR is never slashable and there is no yield for absorbing risk. That mechanism belongs to Yield DTFs, which MAG7 is not.

**Caveats.** Locked RSR cannot be transferred during the unlock window; governance powers are broad, with the consequences set out in Section 8.8 and Section 13; and eligibility to buy MAG7 itself is a separate question with a separate answer (Section 11). One gap is stated here rather than bracketed: only MAG7's buy-side compliance endpoint was probed for this document, and only from a US IP, so **whether Reserve's per-DTF geolocation gate also covers the vote-locking surface is not established** — assume it may, and take the in-app check as the only authoritative answer for your own wallet and location.

All of it is changeable by governance — reconfirm onchain, and reconfirm against the DTF's own **Details + Roles** page in the app.

No public label attaches any of these Safes to a named operator; this document names none.

**Governor parameters**

| Parameter | Value |
|---|---|
| Voting delay · voting period | **2 days** · **3 days** |
| Proposal threshold | **0.1% of vlRSR supply** — 33,691.5569 vlRSR at the 2026-09-16 supply |
| Quorum | **10%**, counting `for` + `abstain` (`COUNTING_MODE` = `support=bravo&quorum=for,abstain`) |
| Late-quorum vote extension | **2 days** |
| Timelock execution delay | **2 days** |
| Optimistic veto delay · veto period · veto threshold | **4 hours** · **20 hours** · **1% of vlRSR supply at snapshot** |
| Optimistic proposal throttle | capacity **3** per proposer, per 12-hour period |
| Clock | `mode=timestamp` (timestamps, not block numbers) |

⇒ **A standard proposal takes about 7 days** end to end (2-day delay + 3-day vote + 2-day timelock). **An optimistic proposal takes exactly 24 hours** (4-hour veto delay + 20-hour veto window) and then executes immediately, bypassing the timelock queue.

**What those thresholds are worth in RSR.** Against the vault's 2026-09-16 supply of 33,691,556.89 vlRSR, the proposal threshold is about **33,692 vlRSR**, the optimistic veto threshold about **336,916 vlRSR**, and the standard-path quorum about **3,369,156 vlRSR**. Set those beside RSR's own float: CoinGecko reported **62,553,174,091 RSR** in circulation on the same date (Section 14), so **the entire vote-locking vault is roughly 0.05% of circulating RSR**, and the RSR needed to reach quorum is roughly **0.005%** of it. That is arithmetic on two published numbers, not a claim that anyone would do it — but a reader weighing "1% of vote-locked RSR can veto a basket rewrite" as a safeguard should size it against the float rather than against the vault. Both figures move; re-read them before relying on either.

**What the fast path is allowed to do.** The optimistic selector registry returns exactly one target, the MAG7 Folio, and exactly one selector against it, `0x207c8eed`, which is `startRebalance(...)`. **The only action MAG7's optimistic path can take is to start a rebalance**; everything else on Section 8.7's standard-path list requires the full vote plus the 2-day timelock.

**One governor, not two.** Unlike Reserve's five BNB Smart Chain AI DTFs, which run a per-DTF governor alongside a shared DAO governor, MAG7 is served by **one** Reserve Optimistic Governor holding both the owner and trading roles, with **one** timelock. *(Reserve's app additionally renders a "DAO Governance" panel naming a separate governor for the vlRSR vault itself, `0x1F01C742c8Dd65e33cEc1aBD6DD049aA245E3453`, with the same parameter values — reported by the app and Reserve's indexer, not re-read onchain for this document.)*

**Fees and distribution, as deployed.** 0.30% mint + 0.6% annual TVL; the four-way split of gross fee revenue, the recipient Safe and the arithmetic behind both bases are in Section 12, *Fees and costs*. One comparison belongs here rather than there: MAG7's split differs materially from the one Reserve's published reference documents record for its five BNB Smart Chain AI DTFs (33% / 67% / 0%) and, in the same documents, for Reserve's Ethereum-mainnet DTFs (50% / 50%) — both reported there, not re-read onchain for this document. Verify the live split in MAG7's **Fees & Revenue Distribution** panel.

**Rebalancing, as deployed.** `weightControl` **disabled**, `priceControl` **PARTIAL**, auctions **15 minutes**, permissionless bidding **enabled**, trusted fillers **enabled** — the "tracking" configuration, and what it means when an auction runs, is in Section 8.5. The app renders the same two switches as "Weight Control: Disabled" and "Permissionless Bids: Enabled."

**Shared operational infrastructure.** MAG7's Guardian contract, one of its two Auction Launcher / Brand Manager Safes, its Optimistic Proposer Safe and its deployer EOA are the same addresses recorded for Reserve's five BNB Smart Chain AI DTFs in the published reference document for those funds — the MAG7 side read onchain here, the BSC side reported there. Same operational infrastructure, different chain, different product: **MAG7 is not part of that suite.**

---

## 9. The underlying: Coinbase Tokenized Stocks on Base

MAG7 does not hold company shares. It holds **seven Coinbase Tokenized Stocks on Base** — the tokens whose symbols end in `c`: TSLAc, AAPLc, GOOGLc, METAc, NVDAc, AMZNc and MSFTc. Everything you actually own sits one layer below the DTF, and that layer has its own issuer, its own regulator, its own fee schedule, its own freeze powers and its own redemption rules. **This is the single largest structural difference between MAG7 and Reserve's five AI DTFs on BNB Smart Chain, which hold Ondo Global Markets `<TICKER>on` tokens.** Nothing in this section is transferable from those documents, and nothing in them is transferable to MAG7.

Read this section in full before you decide what MAG7 is worth to you. The most important sentence in it is in Section 9.3: **redeeming MAG7 returns seven tokenized-stock tokens, not seven shares.**

### 9.1 What a Coinbase tokenized stock is — the B20 standard

Each of the seven is a **B20 token**: Base's own token standard, described in Base's developer documentation — "List Tokenized Stocks", `https://docs.base.org/build-on-base/integrate-defi/list-tokenized-stocks`, which is the live path and the source for every quotation attributed to "Base's docs" in this section (older `docs.base.org/base-chain/…` URLs redirect there) — as "an extension of the ERC-20 token standard and … intended to be asset-agnostic, with tokenized stocks being one of several possible use cases." B20 shipped as part of Base's **Beryl** upgrade, and Base's docs state that every tokenized stock shares the same audited implementation and that the code carries ongoing bug-bounty coverage.

The technically surprising part, and the one that will confuse anyone who tries to look these tokens up the normal way:

- **These are Base-native precompiles, not deployed contracts.** `eth_getCode` on any of the seven returns exactly **one byte, `0xef`** — verified on all seven, 2026-09-16. Yet `name()`, `symbol()`, `decimals()`, `totalSupply()`, `multiplier()`, `WAD_PRECISION()` and `toScaledBalance()` all answer correctly.
- Consequently there is **no verified contract on Basescan** for any of them, no proxy implementation slot to inspect (the EIP-1967 implementation, admin and beacon slots all read zero), no `owner()` and no `version()`. Base's docs say this outright: there is "no per-asset contract and no per-address verified contract on Basescan (precompiles hold no bytecode)." **The absence of a verified contract is expected here and is not a red flag** — but it also means "read the proxy implementation yourself" is not something you can do for these tokens.
- **All seven are 8 decimals.** MAG7 itself is 18. Any tooling that assumes 18 decimals across the basket will be wrong by ten orders of magnitude.
- The `0xb2` prefix is the **B20 namespace**, not a vanity grind; all ten Coinbase tokenized stocks on Base share `0xb2` followed by twenty zero nibbles and a nine-byte tail. Other B20 assets on Base share the prefix too.
- **Identify these tokens by address, never by ticker.** Base's own instruction on `base.org/stocks` is blunt: "The full list of Coinbase Tokenized Stocks on Base. Match the contract address before you buy. If a token is not on this list, Coinbase did not issue it." B20 metadata is mutable onchain — `updateName` and `updateSymbol` exist — so a symbol is not an identity.
- Base disclaims control of the tokens themselves: "Each B20 token is deployed and configured by its issuer, who sets and controls all token parameters and administrative permissions; Base does not configure, administer, or control tokens deployed on the protocol."

The seven, as read onchain on **2026-09-16**. The `name()` column reproduces the onchain string byte for byte; ISINs and approval dates come from each ticker's own ADGM-approved prospectus:

| Token | Address on Base | Onchain `name()` | ISIN | Prospectus approved | `multiplier()` (2026-09-16) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **TSLAc** | `0xb2000000000000000000001e800a7f5189430cD0` | Tesla Inc. | `AE000A4AWG09` | 3 Sep 2026 | 1.000000000000000000 |
| **AAPLc** | `0xb200000000000000000000C2e324d24d7eEcd1fb` | Apple Inc. | `AE000A4AV8C6` | 4 Aug 2026 | 1.000000000000000000 |
| **GOOGLc** | `0xb2000000000000000000002D0BA3164cc74f58B7` | Alphabet Inc. | `AE000A4AV8D4` | 4 Aug 2026 | **1.000377118676784179** |

**Note the checksum trap in the last row:** MSFTc's address begins `0xB2` with a capital B, while the other six begin `0xb2`. A case-sensitive search for `0xb2` across the published list silently finds only nine of the ten tokens.

Ten Coinbase tokenized stocks are listed on `base.org/stocks` as of 2026-09-16 — the MAG7 seven plus **MSTRc** (Strategy), **SNDKc** (SanDisk) and **SPCXc** (SpaceX), none of which MAG7 holds. Some launch coverage reported thirteen tokens; the two official sources list ten, and this document follows them. Coinbase Tokenized Stocks were **publicly launched on Base on 2026-08-24 (reported)**; the onchain record is slightly earlier and more granular — the seven precompiles received their one-byte code on **2026-07-26** and the first four carried non-zero supply from **2026-08-12**, with AMZNc, MSFTc and TSLAc first minted on **2026-09-03**.

### 9.2 The legal stack: issuer, regulator, custodian, and what actually backs the token

Every one of the seven is a security issued under a prospectus approved by a named regulator. That prospectus — one per ticker, publicly linked from `base.org/stocks` — is the governing document and the source for everything in this subsection except the two passages attributed below to Coinbase's FAQ and to Reserve's API.

| Role | Entity | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| **Issuer** | **Coinbase Onchain SPV Ltd** | Private company limited by shares, incorporated in the **Abu Dhabi Global Market (ADGM)**, company number **37941**, incorporated **17 June 2026**, LEI `9845007BEC1A2C555705`. Registered at Unit 12, Level 25, Al Sila Tower, ADGM Square, Al Maryah Island, Abu Dhabi |
| **Tokenisation Entity / CSD / Reporting Entity** | **Onchain Marketplace Limited ("OML")** | ADGM company **17243**. FSRA-authorized for arranging deals in investments and providing custody, restricted to operating a Central Securities Depositary. Maintains the **Legal Register**, the definitive record of legal title |
| **Parent of the Issuer** | **Onchain Marketplace Holdings Limited ("OMHL")** | ADGM company **17246**; owns **100%** of the Issuer; itself wholly owned by Coinbase |
| **Ultimate parent** | **Coinbase Global, Inc.** | Publicly listed; controls the Issuer through OMHL. The prospectus itself flags that the Issuer and the Tokenisation Entity are affiliates, and treats that as a conflict of interest |
| **Custodian and Broker** | **Alpaca Securities LLC** | SEC-registered broker-dealer, FINRA and SIPC member, incorporated in Delaware with its registered office in New York. Holds the underlying shares in segregated Custody Accounts in the Issuer's name |
| **Lender to the Issuer** | **Coinbase Custody International Limited ("CCIL")** | Ireland, company **657718**, incorporated September 2019. Provides a US$3,000,000 loan facility to the Issuer |
| **Auditors** | **Deloitte & Touche (M.E.) LLP** | Abu Dhabi |
| **Regulator** | **ADGM Financial Services Regulatory Authority (FSRA)**, acting as **Listing Authority** | Each prospectus is approved under section 61(2) of the Financial Services and Markets Regulations 2015 and section 4.6.2 of the Market Rules |

The security type in ADGM's own words is **"Certificates over Shares"**, and each token's trading name follows the pattern "Apple CB Certificates". The AAPL prospectus was approved **4 August 2026** and is valid until **3 August 2027**; the September batch (TSLAc, MSFTc, AMZNc) was approved **3 September 2026**, and the TSLAc prospectus cover gives an expiry of **2 September 2027**. The ADGM approved-prospectus register is public at `adgm.com/financial-services-regulatory-authority/listing-authority/approved-prospectuses`, and the prospectuses themselves are linked from `base.org/stocks` — the August batch hosted by Coinbase, the September batch hosted by ADGM.

**What backs each token:**

- **One real share, initially.** The prospectus, verbatim: "The Securities are backed by the Underlying so held, **initially on a one-to-one basis, subject to the Deposit Ratio**, which is expected to increase over time as dividends and other distributions received in respect of the Underlying are reinvested." The Deposit Ratio starts at 1:1 and moves upward as dividends are reinvested — which is why a token is not permanently one share (Section 9.4).
- **The shares sit at Alpaca, on trust.** Subscription proceeds are applied **solely** to acquire the underlying, which is held in segregated Custody Accounts in the Issuer's name **on trust for Holders** under a Deed of Trust executed 4 August 2026. The prospectus states the Custodian is not permitted to engage in rehypothecation-style use of the assets.
- **It is a claim on a real share, not a swap or a note.** Coinbase's own FAQ: "Coinbase Tokenized Stocks give you exposure to a real underlying share. Holders have a direct, senior claim on the underlying equity. Some other products offer a synthetic, a debt claim, or a derivative wrapper rather than a claim on a real share." Each Security represents a **pro rata beneficial interest in the Deposited Property as a whole**.
- **The Issuer is a bare SPV.** "The Issuer is an SPV established for the sole purpose of issuing Digital Securities. It does not carry on any independent commercial business."
- **Unsponsored.** "The Securities are issued on an unsponsored basis: the Underlying Issuer is not a party to, and has not approved or participated in, the Offer or the issuance of the Securities." Apple, Tesla, NVIDIA and the rest have no involvement of any kind.
- **Do not write "SIPC-protected."** Alpaca is a SIPC member, but the prospectus expressly warns that Holders get no "pass-through or indirect benefit from the Issuer's SIPC entitlement in the event of Alpaca's insolvency", and that Alpaca "does not itself maintain or control the Legal Register or On-Chain Data". It also states that the Issuer "is materially dependent on Alpaca in its capacity as Custodian and Broker" — a single-provider dependency.

Reserve's own API describes the layer in four risk lines, served with the basket data and reproduced here verbatim: **"Custodial risks — Tokens are claims on underlying shares held by a regulated custodian, whose failure or insolvency could delay settlement or cause loss of value."** · **"Regulatory & compliance risks — Tokenized equities face evolving rules and are restricted to eligible non-US users, which can force restricted access, halted redemptions, or unwinding."** · **"Liquidity risks — On-chain trading is 24/7, but mint/redeem may be limited or windowed, and in stressed markets token prices can deviate from the underlying equities."** · **"Valuation & NAV risks — Pricing relies on oracles (Chainlink feeds) that can be stale or paused during corporate actions, distorting marks and mint/redeem pricing."**

### 9.3 Redemption and the value anchor — Vested and Unvested Holders

**This is the subsection that most changes what MAG7 is.** In the five AI DTF documents, the tokenized stock's redemption right is described as the value anchor. For Coinbase Tokenized Stocks the anchor is real but it does **not** reach the ordinary holder, and the prospectus says so in terms.

- **Only Vested Holders have rights.** Prospectus cover, verbatim: "Only **Vested Holders** may exercise any rights with respect to the Securities (including any applicable rights of redemption or voting rights). The Issuer will not recognise any Holder as having any rights whatsoever with respect to Securities unless and until such Holder becomes a Vested Holder." And: **"Unvested Holders shall not be entitled to redeem Securities or withdraw Underlying."**
- **Everyone who buys onchain starts Unvested.** The Issuer is recorded as the Registered Owner of Unvested Securities, which it holds "as bare trustee for the benefit of the relevant Unvested Holders". The prospectus's own risk factor (prospectus §4.4(e), "Permanent inability to redeem the Underlying") is explicit: "**Unvested Holders who acquire Securities on unregulated DeFi Markets may be incapable of satisfying the Vesting Conditions** … Only Vested Holders are eligible to exercise Redemption Rights. Therefore … the corresponding Underlying held in the Issuer's Custody Account may remain permanently held by the Issuer and may never be released through the redemption process."
- **Vesting is a compliance process, not a purchase.** Condition 2.4(c) lists the Vesting Conditions OML may impose: completion of all Compliance Procedures; **verification of the Holder's control of a Wallet**; **nomination of one or more bank accounts**; demonstration that neither the Holder nor any counterparty Wallet is sanctioned; and "satisfaction of such other conditions as may be specified … from time to time". OML's determination is "final and binding" absent manifest error. Conditions can also change afterwards: "Vesting Conditions may change even after a Holder has become a Vested Holder, which may result in the **freezing or confiscation** of the Securities."
- **Creation is narrower still.** The Offer is made "exclusively to Authorised Participants", each of whom must meet the definition of a **Professional Client or Market Counterparty** under the ADGM conduct rules and be approved by the Issuer in its sole discretion. "Persons other than Authorised Participants do not participate in the Offer and may not place creation orders for Securities with the Issuer." There is **no published application route for an individual.**
- **Coinbase's public description is narrower than the prospectus, and both are worth having.** The FAQ says: "Primary minting and redemption are limited to KYC-onboarded institutional partners and Authorized Participants, using the same create-and-redeem model as traditional ETFs and ADRs." The prospectus contemplates redemption by any Vested Holder; Coinbase's own marketing describes the primary flow as AP-and-institution-only. **Either way the practical conclusion for a MAG7 holder is the same: there is no path from your wallet to a share of Apple.**

**What redemption of MAG7 actually gives you.** MAG7's own redeem function is permissionless and works exactly as **Section 10**, *How to buy, sell, and redeem MAG7*, describes: burn MAG7, receive your pro-rata share of the seven tokenized-stock tokens. **The escape hatch ends at the token, not at the company.** You then hold seven Unvested positions whose only exit is the secondary market.

**So what anchors the price?** Two things, neither of them a retail redemption right: **arbitrage by Authorised Participants**, who can create and redeem against the real shares at 1 bp and 5 bp respectively and therefore have an economic reason to close gaps; and the **backing itself** — one share per token at issue, more as the Deposit Ratio rises (Section 9.4) — marked by the Chainlink feeds described in Section 9.6. That is a genuine anchor, and it is weaker than a redemption right you can exercise yourself. Say it plainly, and size your position knowing it.

**Redemption mechanics, for completeness** (they apply to Vested Holders, not to you):

- **Settlement methods** (Condition 5.3): **in-kind** — the Custodian transfers the underlying, or fractional interests in it, to a nominated custody or brokerage account at the prevailing Deposit Ratio; **cash** — the Custodian sells and wires USD to a nominated bank account; **stablecoin** — the Custodian sells and transfers **USDC** or another accepted stablecoin to a nominated wallet.
- **There is no published redemption calendar and no published cut-off time.** A Redemption Order is submitted "in such form and manner as the Tokenisation Entity may prescribe from time to time". OML "may reject, suspend, delay, or conditionally accept any Redemption Order", and even after acceptance may "suspend, delay, or modify the settlement" for applicable law, sanctions, compliance procedures, custody requirements, **market conditions**, settlement restrictions, or operational reasons.
- **The one hard cadence constraint is the defined Business Day:** "A day (other than a Saturday, Sunday or public holiday) on which Underlying can be traded and settled and banks are open for business in Abu Dhabi and New York."
- **There are no minimums.** "Creation orders submitted by Authorised Participants are not subject to any minimum or maximum amount", and no minimum redemption size appears anywhere in the prospectus.
- **The issuer charges its own creation, redemption, distribution and withholding rates beneath MAG7's fees, and none of them is contractually fixed** — it may change them on thirty days' notice. See **Section 12.3**, which tabulates the issuer's schedule beside MAG7's own 0.3% mint fee and 0.6% annual TVL fee.

### 9.4 Dividends, corporate actions, and voting — the multiplier and the Deposit Ratio

**These tokens never pay you cash.** From prospectus §12.6(i): "In lieu of making any distribution of cash to Holders, the Issuer shall … apply the amount of any cash distribution (net of any fees, charges and expenses … and any taxes …) to purchase additional Underlying on the Underlying Listing Venue." And, flatly: "**Holders do not have a direct entitlement to receive cash or other distributions from the Underlying.**"

Instead the dividend shows up as a **higher Deposit Ratio**, whose onchain mirror is the token's **multiplier** — a WAD-scaled number (`WAD_PRECISION()` returns `1e18`) that says how many shares one raw token unit now represents. Coinbase's FAQ describes the mechanic exactly: "Through an onchain multiplier rather than by changing your token balance. Your raw token count stays the same while the multiplier scales to reflect dividends (reinvested after applicable withholding and fees) and splits. Your share-equivalent value updates without breaking DeFi positions." Base's docs add the warning that follows from it: "**One B20 token does not permanently equal one share.** Always apply the current multiplier when converting between token units and the number of underlying shares." The helper functions on each token are `scaledBalanceOf(address)`, `toScaledBalance(uint256)` and `toRawBalance(uint256)`.

**This is not theory, and MAG7's own basket is the proof.** As of **2026-09-16**, six of the seven tokens read a multiplier of exactly `1000000000000000000` — precisely 1.0, one token to one share. **GOOGLc reads `1000377118676784179`.** One raw GOOGLc unit therefore stands for about **1.000377 shares** of Alphabet Class A — the Deposit Ratio has already moved above 1:1 for this one name. Coinbase's onchain oracle registry returns the same number for GOOGLc, confirming the two surfaces agree. **Read it as a worked example, not as a return** — it is a mechanism in motion, small because these tokens are weeks old, and it says nothing about what any of the seven will pay in future.

**What that means for MAG7's accounting.** MAG7's basket is denominated in **raw** 8-decimal token units, and those units are fixed between rebalances. A rising multiplier means each unit represents more shares, so **reinvested dividends accrue into MAG7's net asset value without any change to the basket and without any action by MAG7's governance.** MAG7 is not an income product: there is never a cash distribution, at either layer.

**But the accrual is net of two large deductions, and both happen before the multiplier moves.** A gross dividend loses **5.0%** to the Issuer's distribution fee, then up to **30%** to US withholding for a non-US Holder, before the remainder buys more shares. Illustratively and approximately: $1.00 of gross dividend becomes $0.95 after the distribution fee, and roughly **$0.65–$0.67** is actually reinvested at the full withholding rate — the range depends on whether the 30% is applied to the gross dividend or to the post-fee amount, and the prospectus fixes the distribution fee's base but not withholding's. That is a material haircut on the dividend component of total return, it is easy to overlook because nothing appears in your wallet, and it is invisible in the multiplier itself.

**Multiplier updates have no timelock.** Updates are scheduled through `updateUIMultiplier` (ERC-8056) and can be canceled with `cancelUIMultiplierUpdate`; an instant `updateMultiplier` exists as a deprecated emergency failsafe. Base's docs state that admin operations and `updateMultiplier` "execute immediately … the B20 standard has no built-in timelock". The `Announcement` / `EndAnnouncement` events that wrap sensitive operations are, in Base's words, "public notice, not an enforced delay."

**Corporate actions** — splits, bonus issues, rights, spin-offs, mergers — are handled by the Issuer through Deposit Ratio adjustments. Non-cash property may be sold and the proceeds reinvested; subscription rights may be exercised, sold, **or allowed to lapse**, at the Issuer's discretion; fractional entitlements may be rounded away. The prospectus's **"Adjustment Event"** (prospectus §12.5(b)) covers "changes in nominal value or any subdivision, combination or any other reclassification of the Underlying, recapitalisation, reorganisation, sale of assets substantially as an entirety, merger or consolidation affecting the Underlying Issuer", and the Issuer determines the adjustment **unilaterally, "without the necessary consent of Holders"**, binding on all Holders. On a merger or reclassification the Issuer may swap the underlying for cash or securities and call for surrender of the Securities in exchange for new ones describing the substituted underlying.

**Voting: you have none.** Only a Vested Holder may direct a vote, and only "upon … written request … received sufficiently in advance" plus "payment to the Issuer of the Issuer's fees and any expenses involved". The Issuer "shall not exercise any voting rights … except in accordance with instructions of that kind" and is under no obligation to solicit them. A MAG7 holder is an Unvested Holder of the underlying and therefore has **no vote in any of the seven companies.** Press coverage that describes these tokens as conferring "full shareholder rights, including dividends and voting" is wrong on both counts against the prospectus; prefer the prospectus.

**The delisting gap is real and unquantified.** The prospectus contains **no specific playbook for a delisting** of an underlying company. It would fall under the open-ended Adjustment Event definition and the Issuer's discretion. What a token would be worth, and what would happen to it, in that scenario is not specified in any public document — **treat it as an unquantified risk**, and see **Section 13**, *Risks (read this in full)*.

**Termination is at the Issuer's discretion.** There is no fixed maturity, but the Issuer may terminate all Securities "in its sole and absolute discretion" at a date of its choosing, on no less than **thirty days' notice**. A Holder that has not satisfied the receipt requirements within **90 days** of the Termination Date becomes an "Unsettled Holder," and its entitlement may be moved into a Residual Pool distributed **among the Vested Holders** recorded as of the Termination Date. **Read that against MAG7's own position: the Folio contract holds all seven tokens as an Unvested Holder and cannot, as a smart contract, satisfy those receipt requirements.** A termination is therefore a path on which MAG7's net asset value goes to zero while all seven companies are trading normally — a total-loss mechanism that has nothing to do with the share price, and nothing in the Reserve Index Protocol can prevent it.

### 9.5 Transfer restrictions, policy gating, freeze and seize

**Ordinary transfers are permissionless; the controls are reactive rather than preventive — and they are strong.** Base's developer docs state it plainly: "**Holding and trading on the secondary market is permissionless. KYC only happens during mint and redeem flows taken by Authorized Participants (APs).** Onchain policies can block specific addresses, such as sanctioned addresses; a blocked transfer reverts." Base's engineering blog, published 27 August 2026, is the one official source that says out loud that jurisdictional eligibility is enforced as a **transfer policy on the token itself**, mapping "Configurable Policies" to "Jurisdictional restrictions" — not only at a front end.

The B20 primitives, per Base's docs, are: **policies** (allowlists and blocklists, queried with `isAuthorized(policyID, account)`; a blocked transfer reverts; note that the standard `approve()` function is **not** policy gated), **pauses** (per-function), **seize** ("An administrator with the seize role moves tokens from a policy-flagged address to an authorized destination, with a memo recorded"), and **announcements** (public notice, not an enforced delay).

The prospectus describes the same powers from the legal side, and it is worth reading the words rather than a paraphrase:

- "The smart contract governing the Securities includes **blacklist, freeze, and pause functions** operable by the Tokenisation Entity in its capacity as CSD, which remain available throughout the Security lifecycle and **operate without requiring counterparty cooperation.**"
- "Compliance screening is applied to all on-chain holdings and, where detected, prohibited transactions may be blocked and the relevant Securities **frozen or burned**."
- OML may "(i) rescind any purported transfer in violation of this Prospectus, (ii) cancel any or all Securities, (iii) redesignate any Vested Holder as an Unvested Holder, or (iv) freeze the relevant Securities on-chain at the smart contract level, in each case **with or without payment**", and may act "unilaterally where required to do so to comply with its regulatory obligations as a CSD."
- The prospectus's graduated intervention protocol (prospectus §14.2(d)) runs: identification and reconciliation against the CSD Legal Register, with the Holder redesignated as Unvested and the Issuer recorded as Registered Owner; then a **freeze instruction**, "denying the Holder all ability to transfer the Security or receive any economic benefit"; then ongoing intraday monitoring. Where a prohibited holding is detected the Holder is denied "all economic benefit until the position is no longer prohibited (**which may never occur**)."
- Coinbase's own blog states the same thing in one line: "Every transfer is subject to ongoing sanctions screening, and we can freeze or seize assets at the wallet level where required."
- The Issuer also reserves the right to impose further restrictions "relating to particular jurisdictions, persons, Wallet addresses, custodians, intermediaries, trading venues, or settlement systems", to be published on its website and through the ADGM disclosure platform. **Section 11**, *Eligibility: who can and cannot buy*, covers what that means for who may hold MAG7 at all.

**Why this matters to a MAG7 holder specifically.** MAG7's Folio contract is a wallet like any other: it holds the seven tokens at a Base address, and it is subject to the same policies. If the tokens held by the Folio were ever frozen or seized, **MAG7's net asset value and its redemption function would be directly impaired, and nothing in the Reserve Index Protocol could override a B20 policy.** The Reserve contracts have no pause and no freeze of their own (see **Section 8**, *What a DTF is (the Reserve Index Protocol), end to end*); the controls that exist over your assets live at the issuer's layer, not Reserve's. `TKTK[public address of the B20 policy registry for these tokens, and the holder of the seize role — not established from any public source as of 2026-09-16]`.

### 9.6 Market hours, 24/7 trading, and the 24/5 oracle

**The token trades when the stock market does not.** `base.org/stocks`: "Trade tokenized stocks any hour of any day, including weekends and US market holidays." Coinbase's FAQ: "The tokens live onchain and can be traded around the clock including market holidays." MAG7 inherits that: the DTF is transferable and tradeable at 3 a.m. on a Sunday, and so is every token in its basket.

**The mark behind the token does not.** Each tokenized stock has a dedicated Chainlink feed on Base, and those feeds run **24/5** — covering regular, pre-market, post-market and overnight US sessions, and stopping for weekends, holidays and corporate actions. The feeds return **8 decimals**, implement the standard Chainlink V3 aggregator interface, and are read through a proxy with `latestRoundData()`. All seven feeds are live. **The AAPL, TSLA and MSFT proxy addresses below were re-read directly for this document on 2026-09-16 and matched exactly; the other four are carried from a single research source and were not independently re-confirmed.** Check any feed address against Chainlink's own reference-data directory before relying on it:

| Feed | Proxy address on Base | Feed | Proxy address on Base |
|---|---|---|---|
| Coinbase AAPL | `0x787f13dEa48Db0897CbCDD985de77809D837F988` | Coinbase META | `0x6526aE6797A76123638b863AeE4dD27Ba4E4b27D` |
| Coinbase TSLA | `0xFaf869185383a24F8cb00e27BdA6b63B9905DCb4` | Coinbase NVDA | `0x04689a41629776563E6822F76f2e57D148d28513` |
| Coinbase GOOGL | `0x5bF49E0ffA937CE2FfF033c739aD7C634c4D34F2` | Coinbase AMZN | `0x06A8E4b3aBB3B7543d8396FB2B763d22820cB295` |
| Coinbase MSFT | `0xeB10A6c9aa7E537aEd766C08c35Dae35B321b18c` | | |

Four things about these feeds matter to anyone holding MAG7:

1. **They report Total Return values, and the multiplier is baked in.** Chainlink's own documentation gives the identity: **"Token Price = Underlying Equity Market Price × Multiplier"**, with the multiplier read from Coinbase's onchain oracle registry on Base and the equity price taken from Chainlink's traditional-market equity feeds. The token's own DEX price **does not** feed the oracle. Chainlink applies the same approach to other tokenized-equity issuers, including Ondo and Robinhood.
2. **The publish rule is documented, and it is not "continuous".** Base's docs: "**During market hours the feed updates on a 0.5% price deviation or at least every 24 hours (its heartbeat).**" Off-hours — nights, weekends, holidays and corporate-action pauses — "it stops updating and holds the last value, so `updatedAt` stops advancing while the contract stays callable"; Chainlink adds that these feeds "do not have heartbeats during off-hours" and that integrators must "always read `updatedAt` and apply staleness bounds … never settle or liquidate against a frozen feed."
3. **Staleness is observable in the middle of a trading day.** At **16:28:28 UTC on 2026-09-16** — mid-session for US equities — the Coinbase AAPL feed's `updatedAt` still read **13:43:31 UTC**, making the published mark **2 hours 45 minutes old**; a re-read five minutes later showed no change. Feed reads the same day: AAPL **$333.615** (13:43:31Z), TSLA **$362.395** (15:33:05Z), MSFT **$494.585** (13:36:39Z). This is exactly what a 0.5%-deviation rule produces in a quiet tape, it is not a malfunction, and **a document claiming "continuously updated prices" would be misleading.**
4. **There is a live pause switch on the pricing path.** Coinbase's `OracleRegistry` at `0x3f3E8cf41cdd3b1D118c16471aB0113DfDDd5CaD` on Base exposes `getOracleParams(address) → (multiplier, paused)` and `setOraclePaused(address, bool)` behind a `PAUSER_ROLE`. Read on 2026-09-16, `getOracleParams(AAPLc)` returned `(1e18, false)` and `getOracleParams(GOOGLc)` returned `(1000377118676784179, false)` — matching each token's own `multiplier()` exactly. **The registry address is itself second-hand**, from the same single research source as the four unconfirmed feed addresses above; the only corroboration offered here is that the multipliers it returns match each token's own `multiplier()`. Reserve's own risk copy names the exposure: "Pricing relies on oracles (Chainlink feeds) that can be stale or paused during corporate actions."

**One caveat on the feed metadata.** Chainlink's public reference-data directory lists these feeds with a `feedCategory` of `custom` for six of the seven and `low` for GOOGL. `TKTK[what Chainlink's "custom" and "low" feedCategory labels commit a feed to — not verified against Chainlink's own definitions]`. Do not read a service-level guarantee into either label; read `updatedAt`.

**During a corporate action the two layers deliberately diverge.** Base's docs: "**Mint and redeem pause offchain, but the token is not paused onchain, so transfers are not blocked.**" The feed freezes, and because it is a total-return feed there is no price discontinuity — "the multiplier and underlying price move in opposite directions and cancel (a 10:1 split drops the price ~10x and raises the multiplier ~10x)" — and the feed resumes only once Coinbase confirms that both values reflect the new state.

**The practical consequences, stated without hedging:**

- **Overnight and weekend news moves the fair value of seven of the world's most-watched companies while the official mark is frozen.** The tokens can trade at a premium or discount to the last close and re-converge when US markets reopen, and MAG7's own price and NAV inherit that. This is a structural feature of holding an equity-backed token around the clock, not an anomaly. It is the source of the NAV-deviation risk in **Section 13**, *Risks (read this in full)*.
- **Primary creation and redemption at the Coinbase layer are not 24/7.** They run on Business Days, through Alpaca, against the listing venue. The prospectus's "Limited trading hours" risk factor is explicit: "The on-exchange trading of Underlying is limited to specific trading windows available on the Underlying Listing Venue. The Issuer (or the Custodian or Broker acting on its behalf) may therefore not be able to buy or sell Underlying outside of exchange market hours in sufficient quantities, or at all."
- **Reserve's surfaces show no market-hours badge and no per-session trade cap for MAG7** — the session-limit and market-status data that exists for the Ondo-based DTFs simply does not exist for this issuer, and Reserve's API returns an empty asset list when asked for it. **Do not read that as 24/7 mint-and-redeem parity.** The app imposes no window; the layer beneath it does, and that constraint is not surfaced.

### 9.7 How big this market actually is

MAG7 sits on top of a very small float, and the scale is worth stating precisely rather than gesturing at. Every figure in the table below was read from chain and from each token's Chainlink feed on **2026-09-16 at approximately 15:35 UTC**, and **every one of them moves**:

| Token | Supply (raw token units) | Chainlink price | Implied onchain float |
|---|---|---|---|
| NVDAc | 19,081.1861 | $216.19 | ~$4,125,000 |
| GOOGLc | 5,914.0673 | $347.04 | ~$2,052,000 |
| AAPLc | 5,925.3420 | $333.62 | ~$1,977,000 |
| METAc | 2,326.9077 | $677.20 | ~$1,576,000 |
| AMZNc | 5,497.7427 | $248.20 | ~$1,365,000 |
| MSFTc | 2,652.2924 | $494.58 | ~$1,312,000 |
| TSLAc | 2,818.1297 | $362.39 | ~$1,021,000 |
| **The MAG7 seven** | | | **~$13.4M** |
| **All ten Coinbase tokenized stocks** | | | **~$17.2M** |

The supply column is **raw** 8-decimal token units (`totalSupply()/1e8`), not share-equivalents: multiply by the multiplier for share-equivalents, and only GOOGLc differs, at 1.000377 (Section 9.4). The Chainlink price is the token price, which already carries the multiplier, so raw units × price is the float either way.

**MAG7's share of each float is a separate read at a slightly later moment, so it does not divide out of the column above.** On that basis MAG7 held **5.33%** of TSLAc, **4.21%** of MSFTc, **3.89%** of AMZNc, **3.65%** of METAc, **2.87%** of AAPLc, **2.76%** of GOOGLc and **1.27%** of NVDAc — three of the seven supplies changed inside forty-five minutes, which is why the two reads must not be mixed. **Never quote one of these floats, or one of these shares, without its timestamp.**

Three comparisons follow from the table, and each is worth holding in mind:

- **MAG7 is a meaningful fraction of a tiny float.** Its market capitalization was approximately **$390,000** on 2026-09-16 — roughly **2.9%** of the combined float of its own seven constituents. And it holds about **5.3% of every TSLAc in existence**. That cuts both ways: MAG7's own rebalance auctions are large relative to the pools they trade in, which is a real execution constraint and a real reason the basket's costs are not purely theoretical. See **Section 8.5** for how the auctions run, and **Section 10**, *How to buy, sell, and redeem MAG7*, for the pool you would trade against.
- **The whole Coinbase complex is small against the market it entered.** The tokenized-stock tracker at rwa.xyz reported **$2.92B** of total distributed value across all issuers on **09/16/2026** (third-party data, reported, and an estimate of industry size rather than any prediction of MAG7's price). Coinbase's ten tokens at ~$17.2M are roughly **0.6%** of that. Coinbase is a famous entrant to a market it does not lead by size.
- **The ingredients are far more liquid than the wrapper.** Each of the seven has its own Aerodrome Slipstream pool against USDC, with pool TVL from roughly $0.8M to $2.2M and 24-hour volumes in the millions on 2026-09-16 — NVDAc alone turned over about **$16.2m** of token volume that day (GeckoTerminal, 2026-09-16). MAG7's own pool held about **$420,000** and traded about **$5,000** (as of 2026-09-16; aggregators differ by a few hundred dollars). That asymmetry is the strongest single argument for the next subsection, and this document is not going to bury it.

### 9.8 Why not just hold the seven tokenized stocks individually?

You can, and for many readers it will be the better trade. The seven are permissionless ERC-20-compatible tokens on Base with live USDC pools; nothing about MAG7 gates access to them. Here is the honest comparison, on an illustrative $10,000 over a first year, using measured aggregator quotes and published fee schedules as of **2026-09-16**:

The cost comparison is worked in full in **Section 12.5**, against MAGS and a broad proxy as well: on an illustrative $10,000 first year it is roughly **$10** to assemble the seven yourself, **$90** to mint MAG7, and **$130** to buy MAG7 on the pool. **MAG7 is the most expensive of these routes for someone who has every option** — its case rests on convenience and on reach, not on cost — and **minting is cheaper than buying MAG7 on the open market** for anything above a few thousand dollars, because the pool is thin. If you decide to hold MAG7, use the app's mint route rather than the pool; **Section 10**, *How to buy, sell, and redeem MAG7*, has the mechanics.

**What the wrapper actually buys you:**

- **One transaction instead of seven**, one position instead of seven, and one line in a portfolio instead of seven.
- **The rebalance, executed.** Doing it yourself at the mandate's stated cadence means up to 42 swaps a year, each needing a price check, a weight calculation, a route, an approval and a signature. The direct cost of that is small; the real cost is attention and discipline, and the risk of skipping a rebalance in a drawdown — which is precisely when an equal-weight rule does its work.
- **One tax lot instead of many, as a mechanism.** Rebalancing happens inside the folio through onchain auctions; the holder's position is untouched, so no disposal occurs in the holder's hands, whereas a do-it-yourself rebalancer realizes a disposal on every sell leg. **This is a mechanism, not tax advice, and its consequences are entirely jurisdiction-dependent.** Nothing in this document is tax advice; see **Section 18**, *Sources and full legal disclaimer*.

**What the wrapper does not buy you, and it is a long list:**

- **It does not upgrade your claim.** You are an Unvested Holder of the underlying either way (Section 9.3).
- **It does not remove the issuer, the custodian, the SPV credit chain, or the freeze and seize powers** (Section 9.2, Section 9.5).
- **It does not remove the oracle dependency** or the 24/7-versus-24/5 gap (Section 9.6).
- **It does not change who is eligible.** That is set at the issuer's layer, not by Reserve — see **Section 11**, *Eligibility: who can and cannot buy*.
- **It adds a layer.** MAG7 puts a second set of smart contracts, a second fee stack and a second governance surface between you and the tokenized stocks, all of which are described in **Section 8**, *What a DTF is (the Reserve Index Protocol), end to end*, and **Section 15**, *Conflicts of interest and disclosures*.
- **It is far less liquid than its own ingredients** (Section 9.7), which matters most on the way out.

The trade-off is the same one every index wrapper offers — convenience and discipline against cost and control — with one difference worth repeating: here **both** routes leave you holding issuer-backed tokens rather than shares. MAG7's advantage over holding the seven directly is real but narrow. Its advantage over having no access at all is much larger.
