## 10. How to buy, sell, and redeem MAG7

This is the practical "how do I actually use it" section. **Always confirm the live details on app.reserve.org; the steps below describe the general flow and may change.** And first confirm you are eligible (Section 11) — MAG7 is not available to US persons or to persons in sanctioned jurisdictions, and the eligibility rules are set by the issuer of the underlying tokenized stocks, not by Reserve.

MAG7's canonical page is **https://app.reserve.org/base/index-dtf/0xcef8db49e456f872e288e1c042f916e9ced7c781/overview**; the short form **https://app.reserve.org/base/index-dtf/mag7/overview** resolves to the same product.

### 10.1 What you need

- A **self-custody wallet** (a standard EVM-compatible browser or mobile wallet) connected to **Base** (chain id **8453**).
- **ETH on Base for gas.** Base's gas token is ETH; MAG7 has no gas token of its own, and nothing else will pay a Base transaction fee.
- A supported token to buy with. **USDC** (`0x833589fCD6eDb6E08f4c7C32D4f71b54bdA02913`) is the swap panel's default input, but only one of many: the picker carried **several hundred curated Base tokens** on 2026-09-16 (Section 10.8), among them **WETH**, **cbBTC**, bridged **USDT**, **EURC**, **AERO**, **RSR** — and MAG7 itself, so MAG7 can be the input as well as the output. **Native ETH is not its own row**: the app maps the native sentinel to wrapped native (WETH, `0x4200000000000000000000000000000000000006`), so "pay with ETH" routes through WETH.
- To be in an **eligible jurisdiction**. The perimeter is set by the issuer of the seven underlying tokenized stocks, not by Reserve, and Reserve enforces it as a **per-DTF, server-side geographic bar** at its own compliance API — a US IP is refused for MAG7 while the platform-wide check is not. **The in-app check for your own wallet and location is the only authoritative answer.** See Section 11, *Eligibility: who can and cannot buy*.
- No minimum or maximum purchase size is stated on the DTF's own page. In practice a small buy is bounded by gas and routing cost, and a large **exit** by live depth (Section 10.9).

### 10.2 Buying (the simple path)

1. Go to **app.reserve.org** and open the **MAG7** page — verify the address first (Section 10.6).
2. Connect your wallet and make sure you are on **Base**.
3. Open the **Swap** tab (the `…/mag7/issuance` route), choose the token you want to spend — **USDC**, **WETH**, **cbBTC** or another supported Base token — and enter an amount. The panel's default slippage tolerance was **0.5%** as of 2026-09-16.
4. Review the quote. The app's **zapper** converts your chosen token into the seven basket tokens and **mints** MAG7 in one atomic transaction.
5. Confirm in your wallet and pay gas in ETH. You now hold MAG7.

Two alternatives sit on the same tab: a **manual mint/redeem** mode, which hands you the exact per-token basket amounts and full slippage control, and an **automated mint** wizard.

**One structural point to understand before sizing anything: buying MAG7 through the app mints new MAG7 — it does not trade the Aerodrome pool** (Section 8.4 has the quote evidence). So **purchase execution is not bound by MAG7's own pool depth — but it is not unbounded either.** Minting sources the seven tokenized stocks from *their* pools, each of which held roughly **$0.8m to $2.2m** on 2026-09-16 (Section 9.7), so a mint large enough to move those seven pools will show up as price impact in the quote. The largest quote measured for this document was **100,000 USDC**; nothing above that size was tested, and no impact figure above that size should be assumed. Selling is a different matter again (Sections 10.3 and 10.9).

You can also trade MAG7 **directly in its Aerodrome pool** — CoinGecko's own trade link points there (`https://aerodrome.finance/swap?from=0xcef8db49e456f872e288e1c042f916e9ced7c781&to=0x833589fCD6eDb6E08f4c7C32D4f71b54bdA02913`). The `aerodrome.finance/deposit?…` link that has circulated publicly is for **providing liquidity** to that pool — a different activity with its own risks, not a way to buy.

### 10.3 Selling

The reverse: on the MAG7 page open the **Swap** tab, switch to the sell side, choose the token you want to receive, review the quote, and confirm. The app redeems the basket and returns your chosen token, minus fees and any slippage or dust.

**Selling is the constrained side of this product.** Buying mints new shares and consumes none of MAG7's own pool depth; selling is measurably the tighter side — but this document does not claim to know which market binds. The sell route redeems the basket and sells the seven tokenized stocks, whose own floats are small (Section 9.7), and MAG7's only pool is thin (Section 10.9). **Read the quoted price impact before sizing an exit** — and note that redemption to the underlying tokens (Section 10.4) is always available and never touches the pool.

### 10.4 Redeeming to the underlying (the escape hatch)

Beyond selling for USDC, you can **redeem MAG7 onchain for its share of the seven Coinbase tokenized stocks** — through the app's manual mode, or by calling the contract directly:

```
redeem(uint256 shares, address receiver, address[] assets, uint256[] minAmountsOut)
  on 0xcef8db49e456f872e288e1c042f916e9ced7c781
```

Redemption is **permissionless** — no authorized participants, no allowlist, no front-end — and it is the one action the protocol deliberately never switches off: `mint`, `startRebalance`, `openAuction`, `openAuctionUnrestricted`, `bid` and `createTrustedFill` all carry the `notDeprecated` modifier, and **`redeem` does not**. If governance ever deprecated the Folio it would enter redemption-only mode rather than trapping holders; if every Reserve front-end were down, the contract would still be callable. You receive **TSLAc, AAPLc, GOOGLc, METAc, NVDAc, AMZNc and MSFTc** in proportion to your shares — 8-decimal tokens, against MAG7's 18 (Section 5). Two mechanical notes: use the asset order the contract returns rather than sorting it yourself, and expect the per-share amounts to step down very slightly **once a day**, when the TVL fee is applied (Section 8). **One condition sits beneath it, and it is not Reserve's to lift.** Redeeming moves seven B20 tokens to your address, and B20 transfers are policy-gated: Base's own documentation states that onchain policies "can block specific addresses, such as sanctioned addresses; a blocked transfer reverts." If your receiving address — or the Folio itself — were policy-blocked at the token layer, the `redeem` call would revert. **Redemption is permissionless at the DTF layer; it is not unconditional at the layer beneath it** (Sections 9.5 and 13.12).

**The escape hatch ends there, and this is the sharpest difference between MAG7 and the Ondo-backed DTFs in this document set.** Redeeming gives you the seven tokenized stocks. It does **not** give you cash, and it does **not** give you shares. At the Coinbase layer the primary market is closed to you: the prospectus offers the Securities *"exclusively to Authorised Participants"*, Coinbase's public FAQ describes primary minting and redemption as *"limited to KYC-onboarded institutional partners and Authorized Participants"*, and redemption of the Securities themselves requires **Vested Holder** status — *"Unvested Holders shall not be entitled to redeem Securities or withdraw Underlying."* **A secondary MAG7 holder is an Unvested Holder.** To turn the c-tokens into money you sell them on the secondary market, in their own venues, which are themselves a young and small market (Sections 9.3 and 9.7).

Two timing notes. First, **Reserve's app applies no market-hours window and no per-session cap to MAG7** — the session-limit machinery that exists for the Ondo-backed DTFs does not bind this one, and Reserve's own `GET https://api.reserve.org/dtf/ondo?address=0xcef8db49e456f872e288e1c042f916e9ced7c781&chainId=8453` returned `{"market":null,"assets":[]}` on 2026-09-16. The layer beneath it *is* windowed: primary creation and redemption at the **Coinbase** layer run only on **Business Days** and pause during corporate actions, and that constraint is not surfaced in the app (Section 9.6). Do not read 24/7 mint-and-redeem parity into the app's silence.

Second, **secondary transfers of the c-tokens are never windowed** — base.org/stocks says you can *"Trade tokenized stocks any hour of any day, including weekends and US market holidays"* — but the feed they mark against runs **24/5** and holds its last value off-hours. Redeem on a Saturday and you hold seven tokens priced off Friday's close (Section 9.6).

### 10.5 Costs to expect at transaction time

Each interaction may involve the **0.3% mint fee** (on minting only — `mintFee()` reads exactly `3e15`), the ongoing **0.6% annual TVL fee** while you hold (applied to the basket once every 24 hours, not block by block), **onchain gas in ETH**, **spreads and slippage** on the routing trades, and small **dust** amounts.

Underneath the DTF sits a second fee layer that is easy to miss: the **Coinbase issuer's own fees**, borne by the basket rather than billed to you at the app. Its creation, redemption, distribution and withholding rates, **none of them contractually fixed** and all changeable on thirty days' notice, are tabulated in **Section 12.3**, and Section 12 has the full stack.

A public, time-limited **cashback campaign** on MAG7 purchases has run on Merkl, announced by Reserve on X on 2026-08-27 and published at **https://app.merkl.xyz/opportunities/5017634236748171580**. Its rate, caps and window are stated on that public page and changed during the campaign, so **check the page itself** rather than any figure reproduced elsewhere — this document quotes none of them.

### 10.6 Which chains — and always match the address

**MAG7 is deployed on Base only** (chain id **8453**), at **`0xcef8db49e456f872e288e1c042f916e9ced7c781`**. The seven underlying Coinbase tokenized stocks are Base tokens too (addresses in the Section 5 table), which is why **Aerodrome**, a Base-native DEX, is the venue that matters and why gas is ETH. Reserve's Index Protocol is also deployed on Ethereum and BNB Smart Chain, and the bridge page at app.reserve.org/bridge covers **RSR**; **no bridged deployment of MAG7 on any other chain is established, and anything presenting itself as MAG7 off Base should be treated as a different token** (see the three traps below).

**Address-matching is not optional for this product, and there are three distinct traps.**

**First, and worst: two other live Folios on Base carry the identical name "Reserve Magnificent 7 DTF (Base)" and the identical symbol MAG7** — all three deployed on 2026-08-04 by the same EOA through the same Folio deployer, all three version 5.0.0 with identical fees and the byte-identical `mandate()` string, none of the three marked deprecated onchain, and only one with any supply. **Second, a separately issued `MAG7.ssi` trades on Base**, carrying materially more onchain liquidity on 2026-09-16 than MAG7's own pool, so a ticker search lands on the deeper, wrong market. The four addresses, and which one is the product, are tabulated in **Section 13.9**. The operational consequence is the point: **anything that resolves "MAG7 on Base" by name, by symbol, or by governance vault rather than by address can land on the wrong contract** — Reserve's own `dtf/daos` vault map returns all three MAG7-named Folios under the one Base vlRSR vault.

**Third, the same discipline applies one layer down, to the seven underlying tokens.** Base's own registry page puts it plainly: *"The full list of Coinbase Tokenized Stocks on Base. Match the contract address before you buy. If a token is not on this list, Coinbase did not issue it."*

**The routine:** open the DTF from **app.reserve.org**, check the address in the URL against `0xcef8db49e456f872e288e1c042f916e9ced7c781`, and confirm it on **https://basescan.org/token/0xcef8db49e456f872e288e1c042f916e9ced7c781** before approving anything. Do not search by ticker.

### 10.7 Worked examples (illustrative only)

These use round numbers to show how the mechanics and fees behave. They are **not** quotes, and they ignore gas, slippage, dust and everything at the Coinbase layer for clarity.

**Example A — buying.** You spend **1,000 USDC** (or the equivalent in WETH, cbBTC or another supported Base token). The **0.3% mint fee** applies to newly minted tokens, so roughly **3 USDC** goes to the fee and about **997 USDC** of value is converted into the basket and minted as MAG7. At a NAV of about **$103.45 per MAG7** — illustrative and approximate as of 2026-09-16 — that is on the order of **9.6 MAG7**, spread across the seven names at roughly one-seventh each. A live quote on 2026-09-16 for **100 USDC** returned about **0.9644 MAG7**: the same arithmetic at a tenth of the size. Note "roughly": weights drift between rebalances, and on 2026-09-16 the live basket ran from about **13.4%** (NVDAc) to about **15.6%** (METAc) against a 14.29% target (Section 7).

**Example B — holding.** The **0.6% annual TVL fee** accrues continuously and is applied once every 24 hours. On a position worth about 1,000 USDC that is on the order of **6 USDC per year**, realized as a gradual decline in your MAG7's value relative to the underlying basket — comparable to a 0.60% expense ratio on a traditional fund. Hold six months and roughly half accrues.

**Example C — selling, and Example D — redeeming.** Sell for USDC and the app redeems the basket and returns USDC, minus gas, slippage and dust; because the market is one thin pool, **the quoted price impact is the thing to read**. Or redeem to the underlying and your shares come back as the seven c-tokens (Section 10.4), to hold or sell in their own Base markets — the exit that depends on no app, no pool and no one quoting a price for MAG7, but which produces tokenized stocks, not cash and not shares.

**What these examples are not:** they say nothing about whether MAG7's value will rise or fall — that depends entirely on the prices of the seven underlying stocks, which can go down as well as up, to zero. **No return is implied or forecast anywhere in this document.**

### 10.8 Swapping in and out — the zapper and supported tokens

You do **not** need to hold the seven tokenized stocks, or even a stablecoin, to use MAG7. The Reserve app's **zapper** lets you enter or exit with a **single token of your choice** and handles the rest in one atomic transaction.

- **The supported list is fetched live, not hardcoded.** On Base it comes from `https://api.reserve.org/zapper/tokens?chainId=8453` and returned **696 tokens on 2026-09-16** — a curated list that changes, so treat the count as a snapshot. USDC, WETH, cbBTC, bridged USDT, EURC, AERO, RSR and MAG7 itself were all in it, as were all ten Coinbase tokenized stocks on Base.
- **One transaction, atomic:** pick the token you want to spend, and the swap step and the mint step are packaged together — either the whole thing succeeds or it reverts; selling is the reverse, into the single token you want to receive. **Gas is always ETH**, whatever you pay with.
- **Dust:** routing can leave tiny leftover amounts, which Reserve's documentation puts on the order of **1–10 basis points** of the input value. The 100-USDC quotes on 2026-09-16 returned none at all; larger ones returned well under a dollar — about **$0.44** on a 10,000 USDC quote and **$0.26** on a 100,000 USDC quote, the same day.
- **Manual mode / direct call:** to handle the exact basket tokens yourself — full slippage control, or as a fallback if routing is poor — switch to manual mint/redeem or call the contract directly (Sections 8.4 and 10.4).

**One piece of machinery described at length in the sibling documents does *not* apply here.** Some DTFs hold assets whose main source of liquidity is **offchain**; for those, Reserve's documentation describes approved minters sourcing the basket through **RFQ / intent systems** that act as the trading layer underneath the zapper. **MAG7 is not in that category today** — its seven c-tokens are routable onchain on Base, and the zapper returns a single atomic onchain route (Sections 8.4 and 10.2). Do not read the RFQ-minter language from the Ondo-backed documents onto MAG7.

**A separate mechanism, easily confused with that one: trusted fillers.** MAG7 has `trustedFillerEnabled() = true` and points at the Base `TrustedFillerRegistry` at `0x72DB5f49D0599C314E2f2FEDf6Fe33E1bA6C7A18`. Trusted fillers are an **async swap path used by the rebalance machinery** — a filler settles an auction leg out of band under EIP-1271 constraints instead of bidding atomically — and they play no part in your mint or redeem. All **26 auction bids** recorded in Reserve's own subgraph used a distinct one-off filler contract, across 18 distinct bidders, and the registry exposes **no enumeration function**, so the live filler set cannot be read from chain without already knowing an address. **This document therefore names no filler for MAG7** (Section 8.5).

### 10.9 Liquidity and market depth

**MAG7 trades in exactly one live onchain pool:** an **Aerodrome Slipstream** concentrated-liquidity pool (tick spacing 10) pairing **MAG7/USDC** at **`0xe942B86f5c6d4a780fd673317Ee7E565d25aB7F3`**, created **2026-08-24**, `token0` USDC and `token1` MAG7, Aerodrome CL factory `0xf8f2eB4940CFE7d13603DDDD87f123820Fc061Ef`. GeckoTerminal, DexScreener, CoinGecko and CoinMarketCap's DEXScan all report the same single liquid pool.

| As of 2026-09-16, from GeckoTerminal and DexScreener — illustrative and approximate, every figure moves | |
|---|---|
| Pool reserves, both sides | **≈ $420k** |
| 24-hour volume | **≈ $5k** |
| 24-hour unique traders | **8 buyers, 3 sellers** (25 buys, 11 sells) |
| MAG7 market capitalization | **≈ $390k** |

Two cautions on that table. **Pool TVL is not AUM** — the ~$420k counts the USDC side too, which is why it exceeds the DTF's own ~$390k market capitalization. And **single-digit unique traders per day is the honest description of this market.** The only other indexed pool, an earlier Aerodrome CL200 pool at `0x1e8796fefc70c8e2d041d5d2ffbc9dd89f9e197f`, holds dust and has never traded; Uniswap v4's PoolManager holds a small MAG7 balance but **no Uniswap v4 MAG7 pool is indexed anywhere**; and **MAG7 is not listed on any centralized exchange.**

**Supply is extremely concentrated, and anyone sizing a trade should know it.** At block 51,393,279 on 2026-09-16, reconstructed from every MAG7 transfer plus a balance sweep that reconciles to the wei: the Aerodrome pool held **2,123.159429 MAG7** and one other address, `0x5de51858b1bb47e9ab059ddfaa2a8c73ec756d92`, held **1,567.463602 MAG7** — **56.16%** and **41.46%** of the **3,780.365858 MAG7** of minted shares (`Folio.totalSupply()` reads **3,782.770741** because it also counts accrued-but-unminted fee shares; divide by that figure instead and both percentages come out slightly lower). **Those two addresses hold 97.6% of all MAG7**; the remaining **97** addresses with a non-zero balance share about **2.4%** between them (99 non-zero holders in all). **Who controls the second address, and why, is not publicly attributed, and this document does not guess.** The concentration is the fact; the cause is not established.

The pool receives **AERO gauge emissions** through a live Aerodrome gauge at `0x017BB6502758eCF478446e97e66831989bd109d6`. Reserve stated publicly on X on 2026-08-27 that *"Emissions are paid in AERO by @aeroxyz, not by Reserve, and the rate moves w/the size of the pool"*, and in the same thread that *"Providing liquidity to a volatile pair carries impermanent loss risk; it's not the same as holding $MAG7."* **This document quotes no APR, emission rate or yield of any kind** (Section 12.4).

**What this section does not tell you, because it is not publicly verifiable:** whether anyone seeded that pool and who, whether any professional market maker quotes MAG7, and whether any automated liquidity manager runs the position. Sibling documents in this set describe arrangements of that kind for other DTFs. **None is publicly established for MAG7, so none is claimed here** — the pool, its size and its gauge are the verifiable facts.

**The practical summary.** Buying mints new shares and is **not** bound by MAG7's own pool depth, which is why a large buy can execute better than a small one — though it does consume the seven underlying pools' depth (Section 9.7). Selling is the constrained side, so **read the quoted price impact before sizing an exit** rather than assuming the pool's headline TVL is available to you. And the permissionless redemption of Section 10.4 remains the exit that depends on no venue, no quote and no counterparty — subject only to the underlying tokens' own transfer policies (Section 13.12) — at the cost of leaving you holding seven tokenized stocks rather than cash.

---

## 11. Eligibility: who can and cannot buy

**Eligibility for MAG7 is set by the issuer of the seven underlying tokenized stocks — not by Reserve.** MAG7 is a wrapper around seven Coinbase Tokenized Stocks — securities issued by Coinbase Onchain SPV Ltd under one prospectus per ticker, each approved by the ADGM's Financial Services Regulatory Authority acting as Listing Authority; the issuer, the regulator, the Tokenisation Entity that operates the compliance machinery, and the approval dates are set out in Section 9, *The underlying: Coinbase Tokenized Stocks on Base*. The issuer draws the perimeter; **Reserve did not write these rules, cannot widen them, and enforces them at its own surfaces.** One fact belongs up front, because it shapes everything below: **neither Coinbase, nor Base, nor Reserve publishes a per-country eligibility table for these tokens.** As of **2026-09-16** no such list exists, and what follows says so plainly rather than filling the gap with a guess.

**The hard bar is Regulation S**, the US securities-law safe harbor for offers made outside the United States. Prospectus §14.1(a), *"General eligibility restrictions"*, states it in capitals:

> *"THE SECURITIES MAY NOT BE ACQUIRED, HELD, OFFERED, SOLD, RESOLD, TRANSFERRED, DELIVERED, OR OTHERWISE MADE AVAILABLE, DIRECTLY OR INDIRECTLY, IN THE U.S. OR TO, OR FOR THE ACCOUNT OR BENEFIT OF, ANY "U.S. PERSON" (AS DEFINED IN REGULATION S UNDER THE SECURITIES ACT)."*

Every acquirer is **deemed to represent** that it (i) is not a U.S. Person, (ii) is located outside the U.S., and (iii) is not acquiring for the account or benefit of any U.S. Person. There is no accreditation path, no "qualified purchaser" carve-out and no application form that turns a US person into an eligible one: **if you are a US person, or in the US or its territories, MAG7 is not available to you, full stop.** Beneath that bar sits a sanctions floor. Prospectus §14.1(b) bars any person who: (i) is in a jurisdiction where the acquisition would be unlawful; (ii) is subject to sanctions administered or enforced by **the UAE, ADGM, the United Nations, the European Union, the United Kingdom, the U.S., or any other applicable sanctions authority**; (iii) is located in, organized under the laws of, or ordinarily resident in any country subject to **comprehensive sanctions or embargoes**; or (iv) appears on any sanctions or restricted-persons list. **This document deliberately does not enumerate those countries** — six authorities each publish and revise their own lists, and any single list printed here would be a snapshot of one regime, stale within months. One list *is* named in the prospectus and *is* public, so it can be stated — prospectus §14.1(b), final sentence: *"The Securities may additionally not be made available in jurisdictions identified by the Financial Action Task Force as high-risk jurisdictions subject to a call for action."* As of the **FATF plenary statement of June 19, 2026**, that list names exactly three — **the Democratic People's Republic of Korea (DPRK), Iran, and Myanmar** (`https://www.fatf-gafi.org/en/publications/High-risk-and-other-monitored-jurisdictions/call-for-action-june-2026.html`). **FATF restates it at each plenary** (February, June, October), so it can change without any change to MAG7 or its prospectus, and it is **not** FATF's separate, longer "Jurisdictions under Increased Monitoring" statement, which is not what the prospectus incorporates. Reserve's own Terms and Conditions (updated **June 3, 2026**, before the reported 2026-08-24 public launch of these tokens on Base) name a different list again, at their own §3.D(f) — *"…including but not limited to Côte d'Ivoire, Cuba, Belarus, Iran, Iraq, Liberia, North Korea, Sudan, Venezuela, and Syria."* — but that is a sanctions list governing use of Reserve's services, **not a tokenized-stock eligibility table**, and it should not be read as one.

**The honest gap: there is no published per-country list.** Four official statements exist. All four are public, all four are quoted verbatim here, and **none of them names a country other than the United States.**

| Source | What it says, verbatim |
|---|---|
| **Coinbase**, FAQ "Who is eligible?" — `coinbase.com/tokenize` | *"Coinbase Tokenized Stocks are offered under Regulation S and are not available in every market. They are not available to persons in the US and other restricted jurisdictions."* |
| **Base**, product page — `base.org/stocks` | *"Available only in eligible jurisdictions outside the US. Read the prospectus & legal disclosures."* |
| **Base**, developer docs — `docs.base.org/build-on-base/integrate-defi/list-tokenized-stocks` | *"Coinbase tokenized stocks are only available to persons in eligible jurisdictions outside of the U.S."* |
| **Base Engineering Blog**, August 27, 2026 — `blog.base.dev/b20-tokenized-stocks-on-base` | *"Coinbase Tokenized Stocks are only available to persons in eligible jurisdictions outside of the U.S."*, and separately: *"…they undergo corporate actions and carry jurisdictional eligibility rules…"* |

Four things make that negative honest rather than lazy. **Coinbase's answer does not change by locale** — the FAQ text above is byte-identical at `coinbase.com/tokenize`, `/en-gb/tokenize`, `/en-de/tokenize` and `/en-sg/tokenize` (checked 2026-09-16), so the missing country list is not an artifact of reading the page from one place. **The prospectus never defines the term:** *"prohibited jurisdictions"* appears exactly once across it, in lower case, in a risk factor about restrictions embedded in the token's own smart contract (quoted below), and there is no selling-restrictions-by-country section of the kind a European or UK prospectus normally carries. **The issuer has named where a list would appear, if one is ever made** — prospectus §14.1(d), *"Further restrictions"*: *"The Issuer reserves the right to impose additional restrictions on the acquisition, holding, transfer, or resale of the Securities at any time where necessary or desirable for legal, regulatory, sanctions, AML, compliance, or operational purposes. Any such additional restrictions may include restrictions relating to particular jurisdictions, persons, Wallet addresses, custodians, intermediaries, trading venues, or settlement systems. Details of any such additional restrictions will be published on the Issuer's website at https://www.coinbase.com/tokenize and disclosed by the Reporting Entity through the LA Disclosure Platform."* — and on 2026-09-16 `coinbase.com/tokenize` carried only the Regulation S sentence above, while the **LA Disclosure Platform**, `https://disclosure.adgm.com`, is in the ADGM Listing Authority's own words *"restricted to Issuers and Reporting Entities"*, so it is not a place a prospective buyer can read (a separate **public** ADGM disclosures register exists at `https://www.adgm.com/financial-services-regulatory-authority/listing-authority/public-disclosures`, 29 entries on 2026-09-16, and whether OML has posted anything to it could not be determined, because its search and pagination are script-only). And **one integrator has named a second country, and it is a third party, not the issuer:** 1inch's blog post on Coinbase Tokenized Stocks says *"The tokens are not available in the US, UK and other restricted jurisdictions."* **That is 1inch's statement about its own integration**, it has no issuer source behind it, and it must not be extended to any other country. **So do not go looking for a list that says yes or no for your country, because there is not one.** The public floor is: **not the US, not any comprehensively sanctioned or FATF call-for-action jurisdiction, and not anywhere the acquisition would be unlawful for you.** Everything above that floor is settled at the point of use, and by your own local law.

**Who may mint and who may redeem is narrower than who may hold.** Primary creation is offered *"exclusively to Authorised Participants"* — Professional Clients or Market Counterparties under the ADGM conduct rules, an institutional counterparty category with no published application route for an individual — and redemption into the actual shares belongs only to **Vested Holders**, a status a secondary buyer has no path to; Section 9.3, *Redemption and the value anchor — Vested and Unvested Holders*, quotes the prospectus in full. **So holding MAG7 gives you no claim on Apple, Microsoft or any other share:** redeeming MAG7 returns the seven tokenized-stock tokens (Section 10), not stock, and it carries no shareholder vote, no direct dividend claim and no proxy — a material risk rather than a technicality, and one Section 13 treats as such. Holding and secondary trading, by contrast, are permissionless, with KYC only at the Authorised Participant mint and redeem flows (Section 9.5), which is why an ineligible person is stopped at a front end and by token-level policy rather than by an identity check at every transfer. **That enforcement runs in two layers, and only the first is Reserve's.** Reserve runs a server-side, per-DTF compliance check at its own API; both calls below were made from a US IP address on **2026-09-16** and reproduced independently three times:

```
GET https://api.reserve.org/v2/compliance/geolocation
  → {"country":"United States","countryCode":"us","restricted":false,"isVPN":false}
GET https://api.reserve.org/v2/compliance/geolocation/dtf/0xcef8db49e456f872e288e1c042f916e9ced7c781?chainId=8453
  → {"country":"United States","countryCode":"us","restricted":true,"restriction":"geolocation-prohibited"}
```

Read those two responses together, because the contrast is the finding: **the platform-wide gate does not restrict a US visitor; the per-DTF gate for MAG7 does** — and a crypto-only Base DTF such as **LCAP** returns `restricted: false` from the same IP on the same day, so **the bar is per-DTF and driven by the equity underlying, not by the chain and not by Reserve's app as a whole.** The API has a second, qualified-investor code, `geolocation-restricted`; whether MAG7 ever returns it for any country is **unverified**, because only a US IP has been probed. Two steps the sibling documents for the Ondo-backed DTFs describe do not carry over: **no wallet self-attestation modal mounts for MAG7** — that checkbox modal is gated on Ondo assets, and MAG7's basket holds none (confirmed three independent ways, including a live page read on 2026-09-16) — and **no apply-for-approval or accreditation flow is evidenced for MAG7.** The second layer sits beneath any front end: the seven underlying c-tokens enforce rules at the token contract, Base's engineering blog lists **"Jurisdictional restrictions"** among the B20 standard's **"Configurable Policies"** — the only official source that says out loud that jurisdictional eligibility is a property of the token, not merely of a website — and Base's developer docs add that *"Onchain policies can block specific addresses, such as sanctioned addresses; a blocked transfer reverts"*, with the blacklist, freeze, pause, seize, rescind and cancel powers behind them set out in Section 9.5 and Section 13. MAG7 itself is an ordinary ERC-20 on Base and can be bought or sold on a DEX without passing Reserve's app: **do not treat that as a way around eligibility**, because the issuer's restrictions are, in the prospectus's own words, *"embedded in the Security's smart contract and… contractually binding on all Holders"*, a transfer that trips a policy reverts, and buying a wrapper does not grant you a right you do not have under the securities law that applies to you. **Nothing in this document is an invitation to acquire MAG7 where you are not permitted to.**

**Two things to carry away.** First, **eligibility can change without notice:** prospectus §14.1(d) reserves the issuer's right to impose additional restrictions *"at any time where necessary or desirable for legal, regulatory, sanctions, AML, compliance, or operational purposes"* — by jurisdiction, by person, by wallet address, by venue — while the FATF and sanctions lists beneath it move on their own schedules. Nothing in this section is a commitment about tomorrow. Second, for your own case: open `https://app.reserve.org/base/index-dtf/0xcef8db49e456f872e288e1c042f916e9ced7c781/issuance` and connect your wallet. Reserve queries its per-DTF compliance check for your own location at that surface, and **that check — not this document — is the only authoritative answer for you.** A document cannot give it, and this one does not try.

`TKTK[public per-country eligibility source, if one is ever published]`
