## 15. Conflicts of interest and disclosures

In the interest of the same candor Reserve applies to its own marketing, here are the material conflicts and structural facts a reader should weigh. Three of them are specific to MAG7 and have no counterpart in Reserve's other reference documents: **one fifth of every fee MAG7 charges is paid to an address the app does not name**, **the basket can be rewritten by a multisig on a 24-hour clock**, and **the onchain figures in this document do not match what Reserve's own app displays**. All three are below, stated plainly rather than buried.

- **The seven names are a market convention; the cadence, the weighting and the power to rewrite the basket are not.** MAG7 does **not** track, license, or replicate a third-party index. Many Reserve Index DTFs are self-indexed in the same way; some are not — BGCI replicates the Bloomberg Galaxy Crypto Index and VLONE the Venionaire Layer-1 Select Index (Section 14.8). What softens the usual "self-indexed" objection here is that MAG7's roster was not invented by Reserve: the Magnificent Seven is a market label in general use, the theme's reference benchmark (the Bloomberg Magnificent 7 Index) is a fixed, equal-dollar-weighted basket of the same seven names, and the largest dedicated Magnificent Seven ETF is equal-weight too — although it takes its exposure primarily through swaps and forwards rather than by holding the shares (Section 2.1). What does **not** soften is everything around the roster. The equal weighting, the stated two-month cadence, the price bands each rebalance is given, and — above all — the contents of the basket itself are discretionary decisions taken by the parties operating the DTF. There is **no published rule for what MAG7 would do if one of the seven were acquired, delisted, or simply stopped being called a Magnificent Seven name** (Section 7.2), and no external body whose decision MAG7 is obliged to follow.

- **A constituent can be added or removed on a 24-hour clock, by two multisigs that also run everything else.** The fast path whitelists exactly one function — `startRebalance`, which takes the entire token list and every weight as its arguments, so the one thing it can do is **define what MAG7 holds** — and a proposal can come from either of two Safes: a **2-of-5**, and a **3-of-6** that also holds `AUCTION_LAUNCHER`, `BRAND_MANAGER` and the timelock's `CANCELLER_ROLE`. **The same small group therefore proposes what the basket becomes, opens the auctions that get it there, and can cancel a queued timelock operation.** Section 7.5, *Who can change the basket, and how fast*, sets out the mechanism, the clock and the veto threshold; Section 8.10 gives the full configuration; Section 13.15 treats it as a risk.

- **The operator benefits from usage.** Reserve's app and website are operated by **ABC Labs, LLC**, in reserve.org's own words. Every fee MAG7 charges is split between the protocol, vote-locked RSR holders and a third, unlabeled recipient — the next bullet and Section 12.2 have the numbers. The more MAG7 is minted and held, the more the operator and RSR holders benefit. That is a direct incentive to promote the product, and this document is part of that promotion — see the final bullet.

- **20% of every fee MAG7 charges goes to an address that carries no public name, and its sole owner also runs MAG7's operations.** This is the one item on this list with no counterpart in Reserve's other DTFs, so it is worth being exact about. Read onchain on **2026-09-16**, MAG7's gross fee revenue splits **33.33% to the protocol** (funding the RSR buy-and-burn), **46.67% to the Base vlRSR TokenJar**, **20.00% to `0x0E0c281ff05d34729CD764dcFC4Fa999b720407d`** — rendered by the app only as **"Other recipient 1"**, with no name attached — and **0% to the deployer**. Section 12.2 shows the arithmetic, and why the chain's 30/70 of the post-platform remainder and the app's 20/46.67 of the gross are the same numbers in different bases.

  `0x0E0c281f…` is a **Gnosis Safe v1.4.1 with a threshold of one and exactly one owner: `0xb3CF59A5f12cA319861376C5e63Eef4790a42B44`** — which is itself one of the two Safes holding MAG7's **`AUCTION_LAUNCHER`** and **`BRAND_MANAGER`** roles, confirmed by direct `getRoleMembers` reads on the same date. So the party that opens MAG7's rebalance auctions also controls the wallet receiving a fifth of MAG7's gross fee revenue — and, holding `OPTIMISTIC_PROPOSER_ROLE` on the timelock, is one of the two Safes that can itself put a basket rewrite on the 24-hour fast path described two bullets above. **This document names no entity for either address**, because no public source labels them and an inference from overlapping multisig owners is not an attribution. The addresses, the threshold, the owner and the split are all onchain and all re-derivable; the identity is not, and guessing at it would be worse than leaving it open.

- **Operational roles are concentrated, and MAG7's auctions have never actually been open to the public.** `DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE` and `REBALANCE_MANAGER` sit with the governance timelock; `AUCTION_LAUNCHER` and `BRAND_MANAGER` sit with two Safes. On **all three rebalances to date**, the operator-exclusive window (`restrictedUntil`) was set **equal to** the rebalance's own expiry (`availableUntil`), which means the permissionless window the protocol provides never opened — in practice **only those two Safes have ever been able to start a MAG7 auction.** Bidding into the auctions, by contrast, has been open to anyone throughout — Reserve's Base subgraph records 26 bids from 18 distinct addresses as of 2026-09-16. This is a structural fact read from the chain, not an allegation.

- **Dependence on a third-party issuer.** Every dollar of MAG7's value sits in seven tokens issued by **Coinbase Onchain SPV Ltd** under prospectuses approved by the ADGM's FSRA, with **Onchain Marketplace Limited** as Tokenisation Entity and **Alpaca Securities LLC** as custodian and broker. Reserve does not issue, custody, price, or control any of them. The issuer can **change its fee schedule on thirty days' notice** and **impose further restrictions on holding or transfer at any time** under its own prospectus §14.1(d). The blacklist, freeze and pause functions over the tokens sit with a different company: the prospectus makes them **operable by the Tokenisation Entity, Onchain Marketplace Limited, "in its capacity as CSD"**, not by the issuer. Chainlink's own documentation states that the multiplier is read from Coinbase's onchain oracle registry on Base, and that registry exposes a per-token pause flag held outside both Reserve and the holder (Section 13.13). The dependence runs one way: **the issuer's decisions bind MAG7's basket, and MAG7 holds as an Unvested Holder** — with no right to redeem the tokens for the underlying shares (Section 9.3). Section 9 covers the issuer layer; Section 13.5 states it as a risk.

- **One overlap inside that dependence, named rather than left implicit.** Reserve's own app copy names **Coinbase** among the backers of Reserve, and a separate Coinbase entity in Abu Dhabi is the issuer of every token in MAG7's basket. Those are two distinct relationships. **This document describes no relationship between Reserve and the token issuer beyond those two public facts, and asserts nothing about any other.** But a reader weighing MAG7's issuer-dependence risk should know that the issuer's ultimate parent also appears in Reserve's own description of who backs Reserve. See Section 14.2, which is where the app's wording is quoted.

- **RSR promotion — and the candid counterweight.** Reserve's materials promote the RSR token, which benefits from platform usage including MAG7, and the governance share of MAG7's fees is designed to reach vote-locked RSR holders. As of **2026-09-16**, **it has not yet reached them.** The TokenJar held **10.263699 MAG7 and zero RSR**, no one has run the permissionless call that converts the jar's holdings into RSR, and the Base vlRSR vault's exchange rate was still **exactly 1.000000**. The mechanism is real and verifiable; nothing about it is a promise, and this document quotes no rate of any kind for it (Section 12.4).

- **A public purchase-incentive campaign has been running across MAG7's launch window.** A time-limited cashback campaign on MAG7, administered by **Merkl** and publicized in Reserve's own launch thread on X on **2026-08-27**, was live on **2026-09-16** at `https://app.merkl.xyz/opportunities/5017634236748171580`. **That public page is the authority on its own terms, rates and dates**; this document reproduces none of them. Treat an incentive to buy as exactly that — a promotion, not a return on the token, and not part of MAG7's economics.

- **Two addresses hold nearly all of MAG7, and this document declines to say whose the second one is.** On 2026-09-16 an Aerodrome pool and a single unlabeled Safe held about **97.6%** of supply between them. Who controls that Safe, and why, is not publicly attributed anywhere, and inferring it from multisig owner overlap would be a guess rather than an attribution. The concentration is the fact and it is material to anyone sizing a position; the cause is not established. Section 10.9 gives the full numbers, Section 13.11 the consequences.

- **This document's numbers do not always match Reserve's own app.** Read from the chain, MAG7 has run **three rebalances and four auctions**. Reserve's API (`dtf/rebalance`) and the app report fewer auctions than that. **This document uses the chain's figures throughout, and does not diagnose the difference** — the cause is unknown and is not something to guess at. The practical rule: **app.reserve.org is authoritative for transacting, for live prices, weights, fees, and for any eligibility decision about your own wallet**; the chain is authoritative for what has happened. Where the two disagree on a historical count, this document says so rather than quietly picking one.

- **Not independent research.** This document is a Reserve-aligned reference, compiled with LLM assistance from Reserve's materials, the underlying issuer's public prospectuses, and direct reads of the Base chain. It is **not** independent investment research, and it is **not** advice. Treat its framing of the thesis as the proponent's case, balanced where the evidence allowed. Reserve states the risk in its own public launch post of 2026-08-27 — when the basket still held USDC alongside four of the seven names — in terms this document has no reason to soften: *"$MAG7 is a concentrated basket of tokenized equities and USDC — volatile, illiquid, and may lose value entirely. Not an ETF; not FDIC/SIPC-insured."* Every onchain figure here is re-derivable from the addresses printed in this document, and the honest recommendation is to re-derive the ones you intend to rely on. Do your own diligence.

## 16. Glossary

- **ADGM (Abu Dhabi Global Market) / FSRA** — the financial free zone in Abu Dhabi, and its Financial Services Regulatory Authority, which acting as Listing Authority approved the prospectuses under which MAG7's seven underlying tokens are issued. ADGM classifies those tokens as **"Certificates over Shares"**.
- **Aerodrome / Slipstream** — Aerodrome is a decentralized exchange on Base; Slipstream is its concentrated-liquidity pool type. MAG7's only live trading venue is the Aerodrome Slipstream USDC/MAG7 pool `0xe942B86f5c6d4a780fd673317Ee7E565d25aB7F3`.
- **Alpaca Securities LLC** — the SEC-registered broker-dealer (FINRA and SIPC member) acting as custodian and broker for the shares that back the Coinbase tokenized stocks. SIPC membership does **not** pass through to token holders.
- **Authorised Participant (AP)** — the only category of party that may create or redeem Coinbase tokenized stocks at the primary layer; APs must qualify as Professional Clients or Market Counterparties under ADGM's conduct rules. A MAG7 holder is not an AP.
- **B20** — Base's native token standard for tokenized stocks, shipped in Base's Beryl network upgrade. B20 tokens are **precompiles**: `eth_getCode` returns exactly one byte, `0xef`, so there is no source to verify on a block explorer, yet `name()`, `symbol()`, `decimals()`, `totalSupply()` and `multiplier()` all answer normally.
- **Base** — the Ethereum Layer-2 network (chain id **8453**) on which MAG7, its seven underlying tokens, and its governance contracts all live. Transaction fees are paid in ETH.
- **Basket** — the set of underlying tokens a DTF holds. MAG7's basket is seven Coinbase tokenized stocks, held as **fixed quantities per MAG7 share** between rebalances.
- **Chainlink feed / `feedCategory`** — the price oracles that mark MAG7's underlyings. Each is named for its ticker — "Coinbase AAPL", "Coinbase TSLA" — with 8 decimals, updating **24/5**: during market hours on a 0.5% price deviation or at least every 24 hours (its heartbeat), and holding the last value off-hours. `feedCategory` is a label in Chainlink's public reference-data directory (`custom` for six of MAG7's seven, `low` for GOOGL); this document reads no service-level guarantee into either label — see Section 9.6.
- **Coinbase Onchain SPV Ltd** — the issuer of MAG7's seven underlying tokens; an ADGM company, registration **37941**, incorporated 17 June 2026, indirectly wholly owned by Coinbase.
- **Coinbase Tokenized Stock** — a B20 token issued by Coinbase Onchain SPV Ltd giving economic exposure to one US-listed share, backed by shares held with the custodian. Ten exist on Base; MAG7 holds seven of them, each with **8 decimals** and a ticker ending in **`c`** (AAPLc, GOOGLc, and so on).
- **Deposit Ratio** — the prospectus's term for how many shares back one token. It starts one-to-one and, in the prospectus's own words, "is expected to increase" as dividends are reinvested. Its onchain mirror is the multiplier.
- **`deprecateFolio()`** — the only kill switch an Index DTF has. It stops minting and rebalancing and leaves the DTF **redemption-only**. Index DTFs have **no pause and no freeze**; redemption is deliberately never gated.
- **DTF (Decentralized Token Fund; formerly "Folio")** — a fully asset-backed ERC-20 token created with Reserve's contracts that represents a basket of underlying tokens, mintable and redeemable permissionlessly onchain. MAG7 is an **Index DTF**.
- **Dutch auction** — the declining-price auction mechanism Reserve uses to rebalance DTF baskets onchain. Each MAG7 auction runs **15 minutes** (`auctionLength() = 900`) on an exponential price curve, with a 30-second warm-up.
- **Effective number of names** — one divided by the HHI; concentration expressed as "how many equally sized holdings this basket behaves like." MAG7's seven names score **6.00 cap-weighted and 7.00 equal-weighted**, computed for this document from the constituent market caps as of 2026-09-16 (Section 4).
- **Equal weight** — a weighting rule giving every constituent the same target share of value — for MAG7, **1/7 ≈ 14.29%** — set at each rebalance rather than maintained continuously.
- **ERC-20** — the standard token interface on Ethereum-compatible chains. MAG7 is an ERC-20 with **18 decimals**; its seven underlyings present the same interface with **8 decimals**.
- **Folio** — the name of Reserve's Index DTF contract, and the product's older name ("Decentralized Token Folio"). MAG7's Folio reports `version()` **5.0.0**.
- **Free float** — the portion of a company's shares actually available to trade, excluding closely held or restricted blocks. Index providers usually weight by free-float market capitalization rather than total market capitalization.
- **Herfindahl–Hirschman index (HHI)** — the sum of the squared portfolio weights, a standard concentration measure. Lower means less concentrated, and a basket of N equal weights scores exactly 1/N.
- **Index DTF** — a DTF that holds a diversified basket and charges **mint and TVL fees**, governed by vote-locked RSR. Distinct from a **Yield DTF**, which holds yield-bearing collateral and uses staked, slashable RSR as first-loss capital. MAG7 is an Index DTF; vote-locking to it carries no slashing.
- **Magnificent Seven** — the market label for seven US mega-cap companies — Alphabet, Amazon, Apple, Meta Platforms, Microsoft, NVIDIA and Tesla — generally credited to BofA's Michael Hartnett in May 2023. It is a naming convention, **not an index with inclusion rules**: there is no screen, no size minimum, and nothing for a company to fail.
- **`mandate()`** — the human-readable string stored on a Folio describing what the DTF is meant to hold. MAG7's names the seven companies and says the basket "rebalances every two months." **It is a description, not an enforcement mechanism** — nothing in the contract checks the basket against it, and nothing onchain times the cadence.
- **Market-capitalization weighting** — the alternative convention, in which each constituent's weight is proportional to its market value. It is the most common convention among Reserve's other Index DTFs, though not universal — MVTT10F, for instance, is fundamentals-weighted. Applied to these seven names on 2026-09-16 it would put NVIDIA near 22% and Tesla near 6% rather than 14.29% each (illustrative and approximate).
- **Mint / redeem** — creating new DTF tokens by delivering the basket (mint) or burning DTF tokens to receive the basket (redeem), at NAV, permissionlessly. **Redeeming MAG7 returns seven tokenized-stock tokens, never seven shares.**
- **Multiplier** — a WAD-scaled number on each Coinbase tokenized stock (`1e18` = 1.0) saying how many shares one raw token unit represents. It rises as dividends are reinvested and adjusts for splits; on 2026-09-16 GOOGLc read **1.000377** and the other six read exactly 1.0. One B20 token does not permanently equal one share.
- **NAV (Net Asset Value)** — the combined value of a DTF's underlying tokens, per share; the basis on which minting and redeeming happen and the reference the market price trades around.
- **Onchain Marketplace Limited (OML)** — the ADGM company (registration **17243**) acting as Tokenisation Entity, central securities depository and Reporting Entity for the Coinbase tokenized stocks. It holds the blacklist, freeze and pause powers over the tokens and determines Vesting Conditions.
- **Optimistic Governor** — the governor contract MAG7 uses ("Reserve Optimistic Governor", version 1.0.0), which allows a whitelisted proposer to execute a whitelisted function on a short veto clock instead of a full vote. For MAG7 **exactly one function is whitelisted: `startRebalance`** (Section 7.5).
- **Prospectus (ADGM)** — the offering document for each Coinbase tokenized stock, approved by the FSRA as Listing Authority under ADGM's Market Rules. The AAPLc prospectus was approved on **4 August 2026** and is valid until **3 August 2027**; all seven of MAG7's underlyings have one, four linked from `coinbase.com/tokenize` and three hosted by ADGM itself. **It is the governing document for the underlying tokens** — Coinbase's retail user agreement is not.
- **Rebalance drift** — the gap that opens between target and live weights between rebalances, because the basket is held as fixed token quantities while prices move. On 2026-09-16 MAG7's live weights spanned about **2.1 percentage points** around the 14.29% target.
- **Rebalance manager / auction launcher** — the two operational roles in an Index DTF. The `REBALANCE_MANAGER` proposes a rebalance, fixing the token list, the weights and the price bands; an `AUCTION_LAUNCHER` may then open auctions inside those bounds and narrow prices, but on a tracking DTF can never move the weights.
- **Regulation S** — the US securities-law exemption for offers made outside the United States, under which the Coinbase tokenized stocks are issued. It is the reason they are not available to US persons (Section 11).
- **RSR (Reserve Rights)** — the Reserve ecosystem token, used for governance (vote-locking on Index DTFs) and value accrual (a fee-funded buy-and-burn at the protocol layer). Separate from MAG7. Canonical RSR on **Base**: `0xaB36452DbAC151bE02b16Ca17d8919826072f64a`; on **Ethereum**: `0x320623b8E4fF03373931769A31Fc52A4E78B5d70`.
- **RToken** — the older technical name for any token launched on Reserve, Yield or Index. "MAG7 is an Index DTF" and "MAG7 is an Index RToken" mean the same thing.
- **TokenJar** — the contract that receives an Index DTF's governance fee share and converts it into the staking vault's asset. MAG7's is the `GenericTokenJar` at `0xEAfA84184BEb90891cb5c4942ebD59C18dda0bfE`; its conversion call is permissionless, and as of 2026-09-16 it had not been run.
- **Total Return feed** — a price feed whose value already includes reinvested dividends and split adjustments, because it multiplies the equity price by the token's multiplier. A split therefore produces no price discontinuity: price and multiplier move in opposite directions and cancel.
- **Tracking DTF** — a DTF whose `rebalanceControl().weightControl` is `false`, so basket quantities are fixed when a rebalance is *proposed* and the operator running the auctions may only narrow prices inside the approved band. **MAG7 is a tracking DTF.**
- **Trusted Filler** — Reserve's opt-in framework for settling rebalance flow through asynchronous swaps with known fillers rather than atomic onchain bids, constrained by EIP-1271 signature checks. MAG7 has it enabled (registry `0x72DB5f49D0599C314E2f2FEDf6Fe33E1bA6C7A18`); this document identifies no filler or solver behind MAG7's auctions, because the registry publishes no list.
- **TVL fee** — the ongoing management fee on a DTF's assets, charged not in cash but in **newly minted DTF shares**, which dilute existing holders. MAG7's is **0.6% a year**, accrued in whole UTC days rather than block by block (Section 12.1).
- **Vested Holder / Unvested Holder** — the prospectus's two classes of tokenized-stock holder. Only a **Vested Holder**, having satisfied the Tokenisation Entity's Vesting Conditions, may redeem: *"Unvested Holders shall not be entitled to redeem Securities or withdraw Underlying."* MAG7's basket, and anyone holding MAG7, is unvested — which is why redemption returns tokens rather than shares.
- **vlRSR (vote-locked RSR)** — the form RSR takes when locked to a specific Index DTF for governance. MAG7's vault on Base is `0x2F0D6538807a77d4AdDCd4b4DAf214Ea2E818E3D`, with a **7-day unlock delay** and an exchange rate that only rises as fees are converted into RSR. Vote-locking to an Index DTF is not staking-as-collateral and carries no slashing.
- **Vote-locking** — committing RSR to an Index DTF to become one of its governors and to receive that DTF's governance share of fees. On MAG7, unlocking is **two transactions seven days apart**.
- **Zapper** — the Reserve app helper that lets you enter or exit MAG7 using a single token of your choice (USDC, WETH, cbBTC and hundreds of other Base tokens) in one atomic transaction. For MAG7 it **sources the seven underlyings and mints**, rather than routing through the Aerodrome pool.

## 17. Frequently asked questions

**Q: What is MAG7 in one sentence?**
A: MAG7 is a single ERC-20 token on Base — a Decentralized Token Fund (DTF) — that holds equal-weight positions in seven Coinbase Tokenized Stocks representing Alphabet, Amazon, Apple, Meta Platforms, Microsoft, NVIDIA and Tesla, and that anyone holding it can redeem onchain for those seven tokens pro rata.

**Q: Is MAG7 an ETF?**
A: No. MAG7 is **ETF-like** in spirit — one token, one basket, mint and redeem at the value of the underlying — but it is **not an ETF and is not regulated as one**: not a registered investment product, not a deposit, and not FDIC- or SIPC-insured. Registered ETFs on the same seven names do exist, and Section 2 puts them side by side with this product rather than pretending they do not.

**Q: Is this the same as the MAGS ETF?**
A: No — same seven companies, different instrument, different buyer. Roundhill's **MAGS** is an actively managed US-registered ETF at **0.30% a year** (its Summary Prospectus, April 30, 2026) that takes most of its exposure through **swap agreements and forward contracts** alongside Treasury bills, not by holding the seven stocks; MAG7 charges 0.3% to mint plus roughly 0.6% a year, holds the seven tokenized stocks directly, and is far smaller. **The decisive difference is eligibility: a US person can buy MAGS and cannot buy MAG7**, while EU and UK retail generally cannot buy MAGS. Section 2 puts the two side by side and Section 12 costs them out.

**Q: What does MAG7 actually hold?**
A: Seven **Coinbase Tokenized Stocks** on Base — TSLAc, AAPLc, GOOGLc, METAc, NVDAc, AMZNc and MSFTc — and, since 2026-09-09, nothing else. They are securities issued by **Coinbase Onchain SPV Ltd** under ADGM-approved prospectuses, with the underlying shares held in custody at **Alpaca Securities LLC**. Section 5 lists the holdings, target weights and units per MAG7 as of 2026-09-16; Section 9 describes the tokenized-stock layer in full.

**Q: Why does my wallet show seven odd "...c" tokens, and why is there no contract for them on Basescan?**
A: Because Coinbase Tokenized Stocks are **B20 tokens: Base-native precompiles, not deployed contracts.** `eth_getCode` on any of the seven returns exactly one byte, `0xef`, while `name()`, `symbol()`, `totalSupply()` and `multiplier()` answer normally — Base's own documentation says precompiles hold no bytecode, so there is no per-asset contract to verify on Basescan. All ten share the `0xb2` prefix, which is the B20 namespace and not a vanity address, so **match the full address against base.org/stocks before you trust a ticker** (Section 9).

**Q: What backs MAG7, and how strong is that backing?**
A: One real share per token, held on trust for holders in a segregated custody account at **Alpaca Securities LLC**, one-to-one at issue and subject to a **Deposit Ratio** expected to rise as dividends are reinvested. The shares are real and the custody is regulated; **what is weaker than it sounds is your claim on them** — see the next question. Alpaca's SIPC membership confers **no pass-through benefit to token holders**, and the securities are **unsponsored**: the seven companies are not parties to any of it. Section 9 quotes the prospectus.

**Q: Can I get the actual shares?**
A: **No.** Only **Vested Holders** may redeem, and the prospectus states flatly: *"Unvested Holders shall not be entitled to redeem Securities or withdraw Underlying."* Everyone who acquired the tokens on the secondary market — which is every MAG7 holder — holds them as an Unvested Holder, and the Vesting Conditions are set by the Tokenisation Entity, **Onchain Marketplace Limited**, whose determination is final and binding. **Redeeming MAG7 gives you seven tokenized-stock tokens, not seven shares: the escape hatch ends at the token** (Section 9).

**Q: Can I redeem MAG7, and how is redeeming different from buying or selling?**
A: Yes, and redemption is the permissionless half: `redeem()` burns your MAG7 and returns your pro-rata share of the seven underlying tokens in one transaction, with no approval, no queue and no counterparty. It is the one function that survives everything else — even `deprecateFolio()`, the protocol's only emergency lever, gates minting, rebalancing and auctions but **not** redemption, and Index DTFs have no pause and no freeze at all. **Buying** is the other direction, and what almost everyone does: the app **mints** new MAG7 from the underlying basket rather than trading against the pool (Section 8). Selling into the pool is a third thing again, and the constrained one (Section 10).

**Q: How are the weights decided, and why equal weight?**
A: **Equal weight — one seventh each, with no cap, no minimum market cap and no screen**, because the mandate names seven specific companies. That is the **convention for this basket, not a deviation from it**: the Bloomberg Magnificent 7 Index is an equal-dollar weighted benchmark over a fixed basket of seven, and MAGS is equal-weight too. Section 7 measures name by name what equal weighting buys; Section 4 explains what it does not buy — these seven are closer to one trade than to seven.

**Q: Why every two months instead of quarterly?**
A: The onchain `mandate()` says the basket "rebalances every two months", and **that is the genuine outlier in this category** — the Bloomberg index, MAGS, the S&P 500 Equal Weight Index and the filed (not yet trading) Global X Magnificent Six ETF all rebalance quarterly. The arithmetic is **six resets a year instead of four**. **Nothing onchain enforces, schedules or times the cadence:** it is a stated intention in a mandate string, not an observed schedule, and Section 7 carries the record of what has actually run.

**Q: How equal is "equal weight" between rebalances?**
A: Modestly, not exactly. Each rebalance fixes **quantities** worth one seventh at the price snapshot used when it was proposed and then holds them constant, so the weights drift with prices until the next reset: as of 2026-09-16 16:30 UTC the live weights ran from Meta at about **15.5%** down to NVIDIA at about **13.4%**, a spread of roughly **2.1 percentage points** around the 14.2857% target (Reserve API; the figure moves with every read). MAG7 is also a **tracking** DTF — `rebalanceControl().weightControl` is `false` — so an auction launcher can narrow prices inside the proposed band but cannot move the weights (Section 7).

**Q: What happens at a rebalance?**
A: It is opened onchain with `startRebalance`, which sets target quantities, price bands and a time-to-live for every token; trading then runs in **auctions of 900 seconds each** (`auctionLength()` = 15 minutes), filled by bidders against the Folio. Read directly from the contract, **three rebalances and four auctions** have run to date, one rebalance expiring having moved nothing. Section 7 carries the full record, including where Reserve's own app and indexer disagree with the chain.

**Q: Why is Tesla about 14% here when it is about 2% of the S&P 500?**
A: Because MAG7 is an equal-weight basket of seven names, not a slice of the market. Tesla is roughly **2% of the S&P 500** by weight (computed from Slickcharts, 2026-09-16) and the smallest of the seven by market capitalization, so one seventh is a deliberate **overweight relative to the market's own pricing**, just as one seventh of NVIDIA, the largest, is a deliberate underweight. That is an active view, not a neutral one; Section 7 has the per-name differences and the arguments on both sides.

**Q: Why isn't Broadcom in it? Or Berkshire, or Netflix?**
A: Because "Magnificent Seven" is a **naming convention, not a screen.** The mandate hard-codes seven companies: there is no market-cap floor, no sector rule and no eligibility test a company could pass to be added. The convention is genuinely contested — after Broadcom crossed a $1 trillion market capitalization in December 2024 the acronym **"BATMMAAN"**, the same seven plus Broadcom, entered circulation, and it stuck well enough that Robeco described that eight as the largest names in the MSCI World Index at the time. Changing MAG7's seven would take a governance proposal.

**Q: What happens if one of the seven stops being a "Magnificent Seven" name — or is acquired, or delisted?**
A: **There is no rule, and this document will not invent one.** The mandate sets no inclusion criteria, so there is nothing for a constituent to fail; any change would be a discretionary governance decision. The argument is live rather than hypothetical: Global X filed a **Magnificent Six ETF** on 2026-08-14 that excludes Tesla (a preliminary filing, **not trading**), and the group's own originator, asked in September 2024 whether he would still include Tesla, declined to answer and called swapping a name out "playing to the gallery." At the issuer layer, a merger or reclassification is an **Adjustment Event** that Coinbase Onchain SPV Ltd determines unilaterally and that binds every holder (Section 9).

**Q: Who can change what is in the basket, and how fast?**
A: Faster than most readers will expect, and this is the most important governance fact in the document. MAG7's **Reserve Optimistic Governor** whitelists exactly one function on the fast path — **`startRebalance`**, which takes the entire token set and every weight as caller-supplied parameters — and a proposal from either optimistic-proposer address executes after a **4-hour veto delay plus a 20-hour veto window, with no timelock queue**, unless at least **1% of vlRSR** vetoes it. This is not theoretical: **Tesla, Microsoft and Amazon entered the basket that way**, on 2026-09-09. Everything else — fees, upgrades, roles — takes the standard governance path instead; Sections 7, 8 and 13 carry the mechanism, the configuration and the risk.

**Q: How concentrated is it?**
A: Very. Seven names, all US mega-caps, all exposed in one way or another to the same AI capital-expenditure cycle — the seven together are roughly **34% of the S&P 500** by weight (computed from Slickcharts, 2026-09-16). Equal weighting reduces **single-name** risk and does nothing about the basket's **common** risk. Treat "equal weight" as a rule about position sizes, not as diversification.

**Q: What are the fees?**
A: A **0.3% mint fee** and a **TVL (management) fee of 0.6% per year**, both read from the contract; a third of each stream is a protocol platform fee that buys and burns RSR, and the remainder splits between the vlRSR governance vault and one other onchain recipient. On top of that sit onchain gas (paid in ETH on Base), exchange spreads and slippage, and the Coinbase layer's own creation, redemption, distribution and withholding rates, which MAG7 holders meet indirectly and which the issuer may change on **thirty days' notice**. Section 12 tabulates every rate and shows where each fee dollar lands.

**Q: Are there minimums or maximums?**
A: No. There is **no mint cap, no supply cap, no redeem cap and no minimum purchase size** in the deployed contract; the only floor is that a mint must net more than zero shares after fees — the `Folio__InsufficientSharesOut` error — which is a rounding guard, not a size rule. The issuer sets no minimum or maximum creation size either, and no minimum redemption size appears in the prospectus. The real constraint is **liquidity, not policy** (Section 10).

**Q: How do I buy it?**
A: On **app.reserve.org** — connect a wallet on Base and pay with any of several hundred curated Base tokens in the app's picker, USDC, WETH, cbBTC, EURC, AERO and RSR among them; paying with ETH works through the wrapped-ETH route, and gas is always ETH (Section 10). The zapper **mints** new MAG7 from the underlying basket rather than trading through a pool. The alternative is the one liquid secondary venue, an **Aerodrome Slipstream MAG7/USDC pool** thin enough that minting is usually the better route beyond a few thousand dollars and that **selling is the constrained side**: check live depth before sizing an exit (Section 10). You must be in an eligible jurisdiction.

**Q: Would it be cheaper to just buy the seven tokenized stocks myself?**
A: In pure fee terms, yes, and the document says so rather than hiding it. Assembling the seven c-tokens yourself costs roughly a tenth of a percent in execution plus a few cents of gas (aggregator quotes, 2026-09-16) and nothing per year, against 0.3% to mint plus about 0.6% a year — **MAG7 is the most expensive of these routes for someone who has every option**, and Section 12 costs all of them out, tax treatment included. What the fees buy is **the rebalance, one position instead of seven, and one tax lot instead of seven**: up to 42 swap legs a year at the stated cadence, done without your discretion. What it does **not** save you from is identical either way: the Unvested Holder problem, the issuer and custody chain, the oracle dependency, and US ineligibility.

**Q: What gives MAG7 its value, and how is it priced?**
A: Its value is the **net asset value of the seven tokenized stocks it holds**, and because anyone can mint or redeem at that value onchain, arbitrage keeps the traded price near NAV — though with a pool this thin, deviations can and do occur. Each tokenized stock is in turn priced as **underlying equity market price × multiplier**, the price coming from Chainlink's Coinbase equity feeds and the multiplier from Coinbase's onchain oracle registry on Base.

**Q: Can MAG7's price differ from the underlying stocks?**
A: Yes, and for several stacked reasons. Weights drift between rebalances; the token trades 24/7 while the equity feeds run **24/5**, updating on a 0.5% price deviation or at least every 24 hours during market hours and simply holding the last value overnight, at weekends, on US market holidays and during corporate-action pauses — and staleness is not only an off-hours phenomenon (Section 9). The single MAG7 pool is also small enough that its own quoted price can wander from NAV regardless of what the feeds say.

**Q: Can I buy it in the United States?**
A: **No.** The seven underlying securities are offered under **Regulation S**: they may not be acquired, held, offered, sold, resold, transferred or delivered in the US or to or for the account or benefit of any US person, and every acquirer is deemed to represent that it is not one. Reserve enforces this server-side and **per DTF** — a request to Reserve's compliance API for MAG7 from a US address returns `restricted: true, restriction: "geolocation-prohibited"`, while the platform-wide endpoint and a crypto-only Base DTF both return `restricted: false`. The bar is driven by the equity underlying, not by the DTF wrapper (Section 11).

**Q: Who decides eligibility?**
A: **The issuer of the underlying, not Reserve.** Coinbase Onchain SPV Ltd sets the perimeter under an ADGM-approved prospectus; Reserve enforces it at its own surfaces. That is the same structural answer the Reserve AI DTFs give, but the issuer is different — **do not apply Ondo's eligibility rules to MAG7**. The authoritative sources for MAG7's underlying are coinbase.com/tokenize and the ADGM-approved prospectuses.

**Q: I am outside the US — where is the list of eligible countries?**
A: **There isn't one, and that is a finding rather than a gap in this document.** Neither Coinbase, nor Base, nor Reserve publishes a per-country eligibility table for these tokens: Coinbase says only that they "are offered under Regulation S and are not available in every market... not available to persons in the US and other restricted jurisdictions" — wording that does not change by locale — and the prospectus uses the phrase "prohibited jurisdictions" exactly once without ever defining it. The two lists it does incorporate, **FATF call-for-action jurisdictions** and those under **comprehensive sanctions or embargoes**, are reproduced in Section 11. **The in-app check for your own wallet and location is the only authoritative answer.**

**Q: I am in a restricted jurisdiction — how do I get approved?**
A: For MAG7, **no application route is evidenced and none should be assumed.** The professional-investor tier that exists at this issuer gates the **primary market only** — Authorized Participants must be Professional Clients or Market Counterparties under ADGM rules, they are approved and engaged by the issuer, and there is no published route for an individual to apply — and MAG7's compliance response from a prohibited location is an absolute bar, not a qualified-investor code. The professional or accredited-investor route in the Reserve DTFs that hold **Ondo Global Markets** tokenized stocks on BNB Smart Chain is Ondo's rule for Ondo's tokens and does **not** transfer here (Section 11).

**Q: How can a "decentralized" token be restricted by country? Doesn't that require KYC?**
A: This mixes up two layers. **(1) The wrapper is permissionless:** MAG7 is an ERC-20 on Base, the contracts are open-source and governed onchain, anyone can mint or redeem at the value of the underlying, there is no pause and no freeze on the DTF, and no central party can seize MAG7 out of your wallet. **(2) The restriction lives one layer down, in what MAG7 holds:** the seven tokenized stocks are real securities issued under a real prospectus, so their issuer applies eligibility rules — and those rules reach further than a front-end, because B20's configurable policies include **jurisdictional restrictions**, a blocked transfer simply reverts, and the Tokenisation Entity holds blacklist, freeze, pause and seize powers (Sections 9 and 13). On Reserve's side the gate is a **server-side, per-DTF geolocation check** and nothing else: **no wallet self-attestation modal mounts for MAG7**, and Reserve collects no identity documents. In short: the DTF layer is decentralized; the real-world asset it wraps carries the compliance perimeter of the real security behind it, and here that perimeter extends into the token itself.

**Q: Does MAG7 pay dividends or yield?**
A: **No — MAG7 pays nothing to holders, and the TVL fee is a cost, not a payout.** Cash never reaches a holder at any level: the issuer applies dividends, net of fees and taxes, to buying more of the underlying, and the extra shares surface as a rising **multiplier** on the token rather than as extra tokens in your wallet. The mechanism is already running and visible — as of 2026-09-16, GOOGLc's multiplier reads **1.000377118676784179** while the other six read exactly 1. Section 9 quotes the prospectus; Section 12 tabulates the distribution fee and the withholding taken out first.

**Q: What happens on a stock split, a merger or another corporate action?**
A: The same multiplier absorbs it: Base's worked example is a 2% dividend taking the multiplier from 1.00 to about 1.02, and a 2-for-1 split compounding that to about 2.04, with your raw token count unchanged. Mergers, reclassifications and similar events are **Adjustment Events** that the issuer determines unilaterally and that bind all holders; subscription rights may be exercised, sold or **allowed to lapse** at its discretion, and fractional entitlements may be rounded away. Chainlink's feeds also **pause during corporate actions**, and at the issuer layer mint and redeem pause while onchain transfers continue — so the token stays tradeable against a mark that has stopped moving.

**Q: What happens if the Reserve app goes down?**
A: The contracts remain callable. You can redeem MAG7 for its seven underlying tokens by calling `redeem()` directly, with no front-end, no permission and no counterparty. That is the whole point of the redemption path, and it is why Section 13 treats app risk as an inconvenience rather than as an existential one.

**Q: Is it safe? Has it been audited?**
A: Partly, and the honest answer has an edge on it. Reserve's Index Protocol carries five published third-party reviews — Trust Security, a Cantina competition, two Trail of Bits reports and Pashov — but that coverage stops at Folio **4.0.0**, and **MAG7 runs Folio 5.0.0, which shipped on 2025-12-10 with no published audit**; the Reserve Optimistic Governor repository carries no audits directory at all. The B20 standard beneath it was audited by Base and Spearbit, with Cantina and HackerOne bug-bounty coverage, per Base's own documentation. **No audit eliminates risk** — smart-contract bugs, governance capture, oracle staleness, issuer or custodian failure and total loss are all possible (Section 8).

**Q: Is MAG7 on Base the same as MAG7.ssi, or as the other MAG7 contracts I can find?**
A: **No. Match the address: `0xcef8db49e456f872e288e1c042f916e9ced7c781` on Base, and nothing else.** A separately issued token called **MAG7.ssi** trades on Base with roughly **$1.99 million** of Uniswap v3 liquidity (DefiLlama, 2026-09-16) — about **4.7×** the depth of MAG7's own pool, which is exactly what makes it dangerous, and CoinMarketCap's DEX view surfaces it in the "similar name tokens" panel. Separately, **two further live Folios carry the byte-identical name "Reserve Magnificent 7 DTF (Base)" and the identical MAG7 symbol**, both deployed on the same day as the live one, both at zero supply and neither marked deprecated onchain. A search by name or ticker can land you on any of these; a search by address cannot. Section 13 tabulates all four addresses and which one is the product.

**Q: Is there a tear sheet or factsheet?**
A: There is **no PDF tear sheet and no link to one anywhere in the MAG7 interface.** A factsheet page does render at the `/performance` route under the DTF's app URL, but it is not linked from the DTF navigation, so you have to know the address. This document quotes none of its figures, for the reason given in Section 12.4.

**Q: What is the single biggest risk?**
A: That it is an **experimental, concentrated basket of tokenized assets that can lose value entirely** — seven names sharing one AI-capex factor, wrapped in unaudited-at-this-version contracts, priced off a feed that stops updating, traded in one thin pool, and resting on a securities issuer whose Tokenisation Entity can freeze or seize the underlying tokens and from whom a secondary holder has **no redemption right to shares at all**. Read Section 13 in full before buying; it is the section this document would keep if it could keep only one.

**Q: What is RSR, and do I need it to hold MAG7?**
A: RSR (Reserve Rights) is the ecosystem's governance token. You do **not** need RSR to hold MAG7 — they are separate tokens. RSR vote-lockers govern Index DTFs, and a third of MAG7's fees funds a protocol-level RSR buy-and-burn, but holding MAG7 requires nothing else in your wallet beyond ETH for gas.

**Q: How do I govern MAG7? Can I stake RSR on it to earn yield or to insure it?**
A: You **vote-lock**, which is not staking in the Yield-DTF sense: MAG7 is an **Index DTF**, so locked RSR is never first-loss collateral and is never slashable. You need RSR on Base (`0xaB36452DbAC151bE02b16Ca17d8919826072f64a`, the canonical bridged token), brought across through the bridges listed at **app.reserve.org/bridge**, which Reserve does not operate; locking it in the Base vlRSR vault (`0x2F0D6538807a77d4AdDCd4b4DAf214Ea2E818E3D`) gives you governance weight one-for-one and carries a **7-day unlock delay**. The candid economics: the governance share of the fees routes to a token jar whose conversion into RSR is **permissionless and has not yet been run by anyone**, so as of 2026-09-16 the vault's exchange rate is still exactly 1.000000 and nothing has accrued to lockers. Whether vote-locking is geo-gated like the buy surface has not been verified here; **the in-app check is the only authoritative answer** (Section 8).

**Q: Who runs Reserve?**
A: reserve.org and app.reserve.org are operated by **ABC Labs, LLC**, which is not a regulated financial intermediary and is not registered with the SEC, the CFTC or any financial regulator. The protocol is open-source and governed onchain. Section 15 sets out the conflicts of interest this creates, including the fee recipient that takes 20% of MAG7's gross fee revenue through a Safe controlled by the same address that holds MAG7's operational roles.

**Q: What blockchain does MAG7 run on, and what is the contract address?**
A: **Base** (chain ID 8453), at `0xcef8db49e456f872e288e1c042f916e9ced7c781`; its CoinGecko id is `reserve-magnificent-7-dtf`. Its underlying tokenized stocks, its one liquid venue and its RSR and vlRSR governance contracts are all on Base too, and although Reserve's Index Protocol also runs on Ethereum and BNB Smart Chain, MAG7 exists only on Base. The onchain `name()` is "Reserve Magnificent 7 DTF (Base)", while CoinGecko and Reserve's API drop the suffix, which is why the product name appears two ways. **Always confirm the address on app.reserve.org or basescan.org, and see the lookalike question above.**

**Q: How is MAG7 different from the other Reserve DTFs? Is it part of the AI suite?**
A: **MAG7 is not part of the Reserve Thematic AI DTF Suite.** That suite is five Index DTFs on **BNB Smart Chain** — PHOTON, NEOCLOUD, POWER, BUILDOUT and ROBOTS — which hold **Ondo Global Markets** tokenized stocks, weight by market capitalization with a per-name cap, and rebalance quarterly. MAG7 is on **Base**, holds **Coinbase** tokenized stocks, weights **equally** with no cap and no screen, and states a **two-month** cadence. It is the only Reserve DTF anywhere holding Coinbase tokenized stocks, and the line-up's only other Magnificent Seven exposure is NVDAon inside BUILDOUT, an Ondo token on BNB. Section 14 lists the full line-up and where MAG7 sits within it.

**Q: Can the basket change?**
A: Yes. The seven names described here are the composition as of 2026-09-16, not a permanent set, and the mandate contains no rule that would force or prevent a change. Weights reset at each rebalance, and — as the governance question above explains — the token set itself can be rewritten through a proposal on a 24-hour clock unless vlRSR vetoes it.

**Q: Is this document official Reserve documentation?**
A: It is an **LLM-generated reference** compiled from Reserve's materials, the issuer's prospectuses, Base's documentation and onchain reads, intended for the MAG7 page and for AI assistants. The authoritative sources are app.reserve.org, docs.reserve.org and reserve.org/terms-and-conditions, together with the ADGM-approved prospectuses for the underlying securities; **where they differ from this document, they control.** Every figure here that moves is stamped as of 2026-09-16 and will be wrong soon after.

The FAQ is the source for the caveat in Section 7 that more than half of that index's historical excess return is attributed to holding more of the index's *smaller* constituents — a tilt a basket of seven mega-caps does not have.

The source for Section 7's account of *why* equal weighting outperforms where it does: the contrarian rebalancing mechanism, the four-factor decomposition of its alpha, the turnover it costs, and the paper's own attribution of that alpha to short-term reversal and idiosyncratic volatility — which is why its magnitude should not be assumed to transfer to seven of the largest and most liquid equities in the world.

### 18.3 Full legal disclaimer

**For informational purposes only, and not investment, legal, accounting or tax advice.** This document was **generated with the assistance of a large language model** from the sources listed above and may contain errors or omissions. It is not an offer, solicitation, or recommendation to buy, sell, hold, mint, redeem, lend or use MAG7, any DTF, RSR, any Coinbase tokenized stock, or any other digital asset, and it is not directed at any person in any jurisdiction where such an offer or solicitation would be unlawful.

Reserve.org and app.reserve.org are operated by **ABC Labs, LLC**, which is **not** a bank, broker-dealer, investment adviser, exchange, transfer agent or other regulated financial intermediary, and is **not** registered with the SEC, the CFTC, or any other financial regulatory authority. Index DTFs and the tokenized assets they hold are **experimental technologies that involve a high degree of risk**; they may be highly volatile, thinly traded, or **lose value entirely**, and there is **no guarantee** that any DTF will track its intended basket or deliver any particular outcome. MAG7 is **not** an exchange-traded fund, a mutual fund, a registered investment company or a collective investment scheme, and it is **not regulated as any of those**. It is **not a deposit**, is **not insured by the FDIC or SIPC**, and is **not offered or made available to persons in the United States, its territories, or sanctioned jurisdictions, or anywhere its offer would be prohibited by applicable law.** Read **Section 13**, *Risks (read this in full)*, before acting on anything here.

**What MAG7 is, precisely.** MAG7 is an Index DTF deployed on **Base** at `0xCEF8Db49E456f872E288E1C042F916E9ceD7c781`. It **does not track any third-party index**. Its constituents are the seven companies named in its onchain mandate — **Alphabet, Amazon, Apple, Meta Platforms, Microsoft, NVIDIA and Tesla** — held not as shares but as the corresponding **Coinbase tokenized stocks on Base**, in **equal weights**. There is no selection screen, no market-capitalization minimum and no eligibility filter: nothing about a constituent can cause it to drop out, and changing the roster takes a governance proposal — which, on MAG7's fast path, can execute within about 24 hours unless vote-locked RSR vetoes it. See **Section 7**, *Methodology: equal weight, seven names, two months*, and **Section 15**, *Conflicts of interest and disclosures*. "Equal weight" means **1/7 by value at the price snapshot used when a rebalance is proposed**, held as fixed quantities in between; weights therefore drift with prices between rebalances, and the token can trade above or below the value of the basket it holds. The mandate states that the basket **rebalances every two months**. That is a **stated intention in a string onchain — nothing onchain enforces or schedules it**; three rebalances have run to date, on 2026-08-20, 2026-09-06 and 2026-09-09, and that record is not a cadence. Constituents, target weights, market caps and every other figure shown are **illustrative as of 2026-09-16, approximate, and may change without notice**. **A concentrated seven-name basket is not a diversified fund and may be far more volatile than one.**

**The underlying is issued by someone other than Reserve.** Each of the seven holdings is a **Coinbase tokenized stock** — in the ADGM's own terminology, a *certificate over shares* — issued by **Coinbase Onchain SPV Ltd**, an Abu Dhabi Global Market company, under prospectuses approved by the **ADGM Financial Services Regulatory Authority**, with **Onchain Marketplace Limited** as Tokenisation Entity and Reporting Entity and **Alpaca Securities LLC** as custodian and broker. Reserve does not issue these tokens, does not custody the underlying shares, and does not decide who may hold them. They are offered under **Regulation S**, **exclusively to Authorised Participants** at the primary layer; the right to redeem for the underlying belongs to **Vested Holders** only. **MAG7 itself holds the underlying as a secondary acquirer, and is therefore an Unvested Holder; a MAG7 holder sits one layer further out again. Either way: no right to redeem for shares, no shareholder rights, no vote, and no cash dividend.** Dividends are handled beneath the token through a multiplier, net of the issuer's distribution fee and any applicable withholding tax. The broker's SIPC membership **does not pass through** to holders. The tokens carry **blacklist, freeze, pause and seize** powers operable by the Tokenisation Entity, plus onchain transfer policies, so a transfer can revert or a balance can be frozen. Their price marks come from feeds that run **24/5** and go stale outside US market hours, while the tokens themselves trade continuously. Where this document and a prospectus disagree, **the prospectus controls**.

**Eligibility.** **No public, per-country eligibility table for Coinbase Tokenized Stocks is published by Coinbase, by Base or by Reserve**, and this document does not fill that gap with a guess. What is published: the tokens are not available to US persons or in other restricted jurisdictions; the prospectus additionally bars persons subject to sanctions under any of six named authorities, persons in comprehensively sanctioned or embargoed jurisdictions, and persons in jurisdictions on the FATF call-for-action list; and the prospectus reserves the issuer's right to impose further restrictions at any time and to publish them on its own website. Reserve enforces a **per-DTF geographic restriction** at its own API, and the token layer enforces its own transfer policies. **Eligibility can change without notice, and the check the app performs for your own wallet and location is the only authoritative answer for you.** See **Section 11**, *Eligibility: who can and cannot buy*.

**Fees.** MAG7 charges a **0.3% mint fee** and a **0.6% annual TVL fee** (the per-second rate stored onchain works out a hair under 0.6%, which is the figure used throughout). Of both streams, **33.33%** goes to the protocol's platform-fee recipient; the remainder splits onchain into **46.67%** of the gross to the governance token jar that feeds vote-locked RSR and **20.00%** to a separate recipient Safe that carries no public label, with **0%** to the deployer. Beneath the DTF, the issuer of the underlying charges its own fees — **1 basis point on creation, 5 basis points on redemption, 5% of the gross value of dividends before withholding**, with US withholding tax of up to 30% on dividends for non-US holders — which are borne by the basket rather than billed to you, and **none of which is contractually fixed: the issuer may change them on thirty days' notice.** Onchain gas (in ETH on Base) and exchange spreads apply on top. Full detail, and the honest comparison against buying the seven tokens yourself, is in **Section 12**, *Fees and costs*.

**No performance or yield figures appear anywhere in this document.** That is deliberate, it is stated in Section 12.4, and it applies to returns, NAV history, APRs and liquidity-mining rewards alike — including figures rendered in Reserve's own app and by third-party data providers. Their absence is not an omission to be filled from another source.

**Conflicts of interest.** The operator sets each DTF's parameters, deploys it, operates the app through which most people will buy it, and receives a share of the fees this product charges — including a share routed to a recipient that carries no public label. The roster of seven is a market convention rather than a Reserve invention, but the **cadence, the weighting scheme and the speed at which the basket can be changed are Reserve's choices**, and on every rebalance to date the power to open an auction sat with two permissioned role accounts rather than with the public. Reserve's own indexer and app also under-report MAG7's rebalance and auction record relative to the chain, so a reader checking the app will see different numbers than this document. These are conflicts and discrepancies to weigh, and they are set out in full in **Section 15**, *Conflicts of interest and disclosures*.

**Identify the token by address.** Three live contracts on Base carry the name "Reserve Magnificent 7 DTF (Base)" and the symbol MAG7; only **`0xCEF8Db49E456f872E288E1C042F916E9ceD7c781`** has any supply and is the product described here. A separately issued token also using the MAG7 ticker trades on Base itself, with deeper liquidity than MAG7's own pool, and other tokens elsewhere reuse the ticker too. **Resolve MAG7 by contract address — never by name, symbol, ticker search or governance vault.**

None of the seven companies, and no index provider, exchange, data vendor or research house named in this document, sponsors, endorses, or is otherwise involved with MAG7; their names, tickers and marks are used for identification only. Full terms, eligibility criteria and risk disclosures apply and are available at **https://reserve.org/terms-and-conditions**. Where this document conflicts with Reserve's official sources — **app.reserve.org**, **docs.reserve.org** and **reserve.org/terms-and-conditions** — or with the issuer's prospectuses, **those sources control**. **"Reserve" is a registered trademark of ABC Labs, LLC.** © ABC Labs, LLC.
